{"parentUuid":"f3af7220-41b2-4cd6-a892-9c1bd139685e","isSidechain":false,"message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-5-5","id":"msg_011Cfi7rqtCLoLvdGnNPvLdD","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"I've written 12 client terms to `CONTEXT.md`: Head Coach, Second Editor, Team Schedule, Alumni Events, Event, Team Update, Alumni Network, Listing, Opt-in, Consent date, Alumnus and Publish. That's above the rubric's bar of 8.\n\nYour answers also changed the design:\n- **Pages:** Team Schedule, Alumni Events and Alumni Network are all pages in the nav.\n- **Posts:** Events and Team Updates are posts.\n- **Listings:** the contact list is a third content type with a consent date. The manual's two types (Posts and Pages) don't cover it, so it's an extension.\n- **Joe:** he publishes his own sections without approval and can't touch the Team Schedule.\n- **Operator:** you run publish in the demo. Matt is the intended operator in real life.\n\nThe next round covers what Q3(c) leaves open, plus the permission model, the schedule's structure and scope.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q6** - **With names public and emails private, how does a visitor actually reach an alumnus?**\n\nYour Q3(c) answer leaves a gap: if the site shows only names, nobody can contact anyone. Options:\n- **(a)** A single \"Contact the Alumni Network\" line with Joe's email. Visitors ask Joe, and Joe forwards the message to the alumnus.\n- **(b)** Visitors email Matt, using the head-coach address that's already public for the team.\n- **(c)** No contact path. The list is for recognition only, and alumni still \"gather minimal information\" through each other.\n\n➡️ **(a)**. Joe runs the network and already records consent, so he's the natural go-between. The email is stored as data on the Listing for Joe's use and is never rendered into `site/`. That also makes the \"Only published content reaches `site/`\" rule a testable assertion. The Joe address on the public page would be a single setting, not part of any Listing.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q7** - **Is the Team Schedule a hand-edited Markdown page, or a structured list of games?**\n\nYour brief says alumni want the \"current team's schedule\" quickly. Options:\n- **(a)** One Markdown page. Matt edits a table of dates, opponents and locations by hand. This is simple and uses the existing Pages model.\n- **(b)** A separate Game content type, with date, opponent, home/away and result, rendered into a table and sorted by date. It's easier to keep consistent, but it's a fourth type to build, test and secure.\n\n➡️ **(a)**. Matt \"knows how to work through a website\", and a Markdown table is the one syntax he'd have to learn. The editor will have a preview, and a seeded template table will give him something to copy. If he gets the table syntax wrong it only affects one page.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q8** - **How do we enforce \"Joe can't edit the Team Schedule\"?**\n\nThe manual says the admin and editor roles need \"genuinely different permissions\", and Joe also can't use user management. Options:\n- **(a)** A flag on content, \"admin-only\", set on the Team Schedule page. Editors can't open, edit, publish or delete anything with that flag, and the flag is only changeable by an admin.\n- **(b)** A hard-coded rule: the page with the slug `team-schedule` is protected.\n- **(c)** Per-user permission lists, where each editor has a list of pages they may edit. This is more flexible, but it's a permissions UI.\n\n➡️ **(a)**. It's one Boolean on a page and one check in every edit route. The Team Schedule is created with the flag on, and an editor sees it in the list but gets a refusal when they try to open it. That refusal page is also one of the six required screenshots (editor-denied).\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q9** - **Can Joe see and edit Matt's other pages and posts, such as Team Updates and About?**\n\nMatt \"has the power to edit everything\", and Joe is \"unable to edit the current team schedule\". The brief doesn't say whether Joe can edit Team Updates or About.\n- **(a)** Joe can edit everything except admin-only content. He has the same access as Matt apart from the Team Schedule and user management.\n- **(b)** Joe can edit only Alumni Events, Alumni Network, and his own posts. He has no access to anything Matt wrote.\n- **(c)** Joe can edit Team Updates but not About.\n\n➡️ **(a)**. It's the simplest rule and matches the brief, which says Joe has \"the ability to update alumni network and events\". Matt can lock any other page with the flag from Q8. Say so if there's something besides the schedule that Matt would never want Joe to change.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q10** - **What is explicitly out of scope?** (This goes into the spec's out-of-scope list, which the rubric checks.)\n\n➡️ I'd list: a public sign-up form for alumni, alumni accounts or logins, a hosted admin on the internet, scheduled or recurring events, RSVPs, comments, media uploads, a structured game and results database, and search. Strike or add items to fit your client.\n\n---\n\nAnswer in any order, and override anything that doesn't fit Matt and Joe."}],"container":null,"stop_reason":"end_turn","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":746,"cache_read_input_tokens":71756,"output_tokens":1699,"output_tokens_details":{"thinking_tokens":0},"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":746,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0},"inference_geo":"not_available","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":1699,"cache_read_input_tokens":71756,"cache_creation_input_tokens":746,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":746},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"serverClassifierRequest":"e5590acb-1e0a-4388-8be8-507191e83705","apiBlockIndex":0,"requestId":"req_011Cfi7rqdZjJWE2dcbetyZs","type":"assistant","uuid":"87446fa9-5b57-41f7-8c32-1ec289c70ba1","timestamp":"2026-10-05T00:09:57.033Z","effort":"medium","perTurnEffort":"medium","session_id":"9d3ee875-b856-4c5f-8263-d5e667d38523","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/sphod/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"d6bafc9f-93fa-4ed5-bad2-bdfd45ad6274","version":"2.1.284","gitBranch":"main"}

Holds the *admin* role and can edit everything, including the Team Schedule.\n\n**Second Editor (Joe)**: Holds the *editor* role. Can write and publish Alumni Events and Alumni Network content, but can never edit the Team Schedule.\n\n**Team Schedule**: The current team's schedule, a single page in the public navigation. Only the Head Coach can edit it.\n\n**Alumni Events**: The public section for alumni gatherings. It is a page in the navigation that lists individual Event posts.\n\n**Event**: A single announcement (for example a reunion or game-watch) written as a post. It appears on Alumni Events.\n\n**Team Update**: A post with news about the current team, aimed at fans, parents, students and alumni.\n\n**Alumni Network**: The public section listing alumni who have chosen to be listed. Shows names only; emails are never published.\n\n**Listing**: One alumnus's entry in the Alumni Network. Holds name, email, optional class year, consent date, and who recorded it. Only listings with consent reach the public site.\n\n**Opt-in**: An alumnus agrees to be listed. Alumni cannot log in, so the Second Editor records the consent when the alumnus asks to be listed.\n\n**Consent date**: When an alumnus asked to be listed. Stored on the Listing.\n\n**Alumnus**: A former player on the varsity team. Never a user of the admin console.\n\n**Publish**: The explicit step that turns published content into the public site. Edits are not live until the Head Coach (or the builder, in the demo) runs it.\n","structuredPatch":[{"oldStart":24,"oldLines":4,"newStart":24,"newLines":8,"lines":[" "," **Alumnus**: A former player on the varsity team. Never a user of the admin console."," ","+**Locked**: A page only the Head Coach may change. The Team Schedule is Locked. The Second Editor can open a Locked page to read it, but cannot edit, publish, unpublish or delete it. Only the Head Coach can lock or unlock a page.","+","+**Alumni Contact Line**: The single public line on the Alumni Network page telling visitors to email the Second Editor, who then introduces them to the alumnus. It is the only way to reach an alumnus through the site.","+"," **Publish**: The explicit step that turns published content into the public site. Three things are true here:\n- The public site is static, so there's no server to run a search.\n- The only way to search on it is a small client-side filter, a text box that hides non-matching names with a few lines of JS. It works because the names are already in the page.\n- The rubric counts \"site search\" as a stretch goal worth 1 extra point. It needs its own `stretch` issue, passing tests and a review comment, and only counts if you're already at 70 or above.\n\nOptions:\n- **(a)** In scope as a core feature: a name filter on the Alumni Network page.\n- **(b)** A stretch ticket, built only after the 7 capabilities are done and green.\n- **(c)** Out of scope. Visitors scroll the list.\n\n➡️ **(b)**. Your brief's ideal is that alumni \"find information… much quicker\", and the list is the one place a filter helps. But the core seven are what Pass/No-Pass depends on, and the filter is a small, separable ticket. If you'd rather commit to it now, (a) is fine, and I'd make it the last core ticket.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q12** - **Confirming the out-of-scope list as I'll write it into the spec.**\n\nOut of scope:\n- alumni accounts or logins, because the public site is static\n- comments\n- media uploads\n- a public sign-up form\n- a hosted admin console on the internet\n- RSVPs\n- scheduled or recurring events\n- a structured game and results database\n\nName search is whichever way you answer Q11. I added the last four from my earlier list, since you didn't object to them.\n\n➡️ Yes, with one wording change: say \"RSVPs\" are out because the static site can't take submissions. Tell me if you want any of the four I added moved back in.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q13** - **Do Events and Team Updates need anything beyond a title, a Markdown body and a draft/published status?**\n\nThe manual's Post fields are title, slug, Markdown body, status, author and timestamps. An Event naturally has a date and a place, like \"Reunion, Oct 18, Gambier\". Options:\n- **(a)** Put the date and place in the body text and in the title. The post keeps only the manual's fields.\n- **(b)** Add two optional fields to Event posts: an event date and a location. The Alumni Events page can then sort upcoming events by date.\n\n➡️ **(a)**. Joe writes \"Homecoming Reunion, Oct 18\" in the title and the details in the body. The Alumni Events page lists posts newest first by publish date. It's less structured, but it's fewer fields to build and test, and it avoids the \"past events still showing as upcoming\" problem that a static site makes worse, since nothing re-renders until someone runs `cms publish`.\n\n---\n\nOnce these are settled, the tree has few branches left: where the Alumni Events page gets its list, and what the home page shows. {"parentUuid":"b1c7b61b-f13d-4ea9-b94f-55d4e937e68b","isSidechain":false,"message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-5-5","id":"msg_011Cfi8yGvrjPb41xBhs7KW6","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Search is out, as you chose. The 12-item scope list stands, with name search added to it, and Events and Team Updates stay as plain posts. No new glossary terms came out of this round, so `CONTEXT.md` is unchanged.\n\nYour answers leave a few mechanics to settle. Most are small and I've given a default for each. I also have one ADR to offer at the end.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q14** - **How does the site tell an Event from a Team Update, if both are posts with the same fields?**\n\nThe Alumni Events page should list only Events, and the home page or a news area should show Team Updates. With no extra fields, something has to separate them. Options:\n- **(a)** A single required \"Type\" choice on every post: *Event* or *Team Update*. It's a dropdown in the editor and a filter in the content list, which also covers the manual's \"list filters by status or type\".\n- **(b)** Two separate tables, one per kind.\n- **(c)** A title prefix such as \"Event:\". It's fragile.\n\n➡️ **(a)**. It's one field on the Post model and one filter on the content list. Joe's editor view defaults to Event. He can pick Team Update, because Q9 gave him access to everything except Locked content. A Team Update appears on the home page. An Event appears on the home page and on Alumni Events.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q15** - **What does the home page show?**\n\n- **(a)** A short welcome, the latest three Team Updates, and the next few Events by publish date, with links to Team Schedule, Alumni Events and Alumni Network.\n- **(b)** Just a welcome paragraph and the nav.\n- **(c)** The Team Schedule is the home page.\n\n➡️ **(a)**. It matches what you said alumni are trying to find: \"updates on the team and upcoming schedules and events\". The welcome text is an ordinary page called Home, which both editors can change. That's why I'd keep the Team Schedule as its own Locked page rather than making it the front page.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q16** - **How does the Alumni Network page get its names?**\n\nThe Alumni Network page is a page in the nav, but the names come from Listings. Options:\n- **(a)** The page has its own Markdown intro text, which carries the Alumni Contact Line. At publish time the export adds an auto-generated, alphabetical list of the consented Listings' names beneath it.\n- **(b)** Joe pastes the names into the page by hand. This breaks the \"opt-in is a field\" rule and would leave removed names behind.\n\n➡️ **(a)**. If Joe removes a Listing or withdraws its consent, the name disappears at the next publish and nothing is left in the page text. The email field is never read by the exporter, so a test can assert that no email address appears anywhere in `site/`.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q17** - **What are the user-management specifics?**\n\nThe manual requires that an admin can create users, change roles and deactivate them, and that a deactivated user can't log in. For your client:\n- Matt is the seeded admin, and Joe is the seeded editor in the demo data.\n- There are only two real users, so I'd keep this minimal: a Users screen with create, change role, and deactivate/reactivate.\n- Two rules: Matt can't deactivate or demote himself, so the site can't end up with no admin, and nobody can be hard-deleted, only deactivated, so a Listing or post's \"recorded by\" or author never points at a missing person.\n\n➡️ Yes to both rules. The first needs a test, and the second is a natural fit for a deactivate-only design. Say so if Matt should be able to delete Joe's account outright.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q18** - **Should I write an ADR for the Listing and its email privacy?**\n\nThe skill reserves ADRs for decisions that are hard to reverse, surprising without context, and a real trade-off. This one qualifies on all three:\n- **Hard to reverse:** once Listings are a separate type, with emails kept out of the export, the data model and the exporter both depend on it.\n- **Surprising:** the manual's content types are Posts and Pages, and an extra type needs a reason.\n- **A real trade-off:** we weighed a public email list, obfuscated emails, and names only.\n\n➡️ Yes, one ADR, \"ADR-003: Alumni Listings are a separate type; emails are stored but never published\". Everything else we've settled belongs in the glossary or the spec.\n\n---\n\nWhen you've answered, I'll check the frontier. If nothing new opens, I'll give you a short summary of every decision and ask you to confirm we've reached shared understanding. _available","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":1263,"cache_read_input_tokens":78120,"cache_creation_input_tokens":84,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":84},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"serverClassifierRequest":"bd27bd82-a9c1-4fbd-a662-464fd029cbda","apiBlockIndex":0,"requestId":"req_011Cfi9TSJD4y7AumzvinaqQ","type":"assistant","uuid":"7a174fb3-b554-4288-acf8-843916ce1278","timestamp":"2026-10-05T00:30:40.945Z","effort":"medium","perTurnEffort":"medium","session_id":"9d3ee875-b856-4c5f-8263-d5e667d38523","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/sphod/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"d6bafc9f-93fa-4ed5-bad2-bdfd45ad6274","version":"2.1.284","gitBranch":"main"}
{"parentUuid":"7a174fb3-b554-4288-acf8-843916ce1278","isSidechain":false,"message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-5-5","id":"msg_011Cfi9TSnU24uAazJDqQ32m","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01NCXDM1vdZ6abowCpHNpfMx","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/home/sphod/code/iphs400-mp2-cms/docs/adr/ADR-003-alumni-listings-emails-never-published.md","content":"# ADR-003: Alumni Listings are a separate type; emails are stored but never published\n\n**Status:** accepted\n**Date:** 2026-10-04\n\n## Context\n\nThe client wants an opt-in alumni contact list. Alumni cannot log in, so the Second\nEditor records each alumnus's consent. The public site is static on GitHub Pages:\nanything published is readable by anyone, including scrapers. The manual's content\ntypes are Posts and Pages, and neither has a field for consent. Options considered:\na public list with emails, obfuscated emails, names only with a contact path, and a\nMarkdown page typed by hand.\n\n## Decision\n\nAn alumnus's entry is a **Listing**, a third content type with name, email, optional\nclass year, consent date, and who recorded it. Only Listings with consent are\nexported, and only their **names** reach `site/`. Emails stay in the database. The\npublic Alumni Network page carries one Alumni Contact Line telling visitors to email\nthe Second Editor, who introduces them to the alumnus.\n\n## Consequences\n\n- The exporter never reads the email field, so a test can assert that no alumnus\n  email appears anywhere in `site/`.\n- Removing a Listing or withdrawing consent removes the name at the next publish,\n  with no leftover text in a page body.\n- An extra model, admin screen, and exporter step are needed beyond Posts and Pages.\n- Visitors cannot contact an alumnus directly. The Second Editor is a manual go-between.\n- Once names are published to GitHub Pages they may be cached or indexed, so\n  withdrawal cannot recall a name already published.\n\n## Why this is an ADR\n\nIt is hard to reverse (the model and the exporter both depend on it), surprising\nwithout context (the manual lists only Posts and Pages), and the result of a real\ntrade-off between findability and the privacy the client asked for.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"container":null,"stop_reason":"tool_use","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":84,"cache_read_input_tokens":78120,"output_tokens":1263,"output_tokens_details":{"thinking_tokens":85},"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":84,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0},"inference_geo":"not_available","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":1263,"cache_read_input_tokens":78120,"cache_creation_input_tokens":84,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":84},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"wireToolInputs":{"toolu_01NCXDM1vdZ6abowCpHNpfMx":{"file_path":"/home/sphod/code/iphs400-mp2-cms/docs/adr/ADR-003-alumni-listings-emails-never-published.md","content":"# ADR-003: Alumni Listings are a separate type; emails are stored but never published\n\n**Status:** accepted\n**Date:** 2026-10-04\n\n## Context\n\nThe client wants an opt-in alumni contact list. Alumni cannot log in, so the Second\nEditor records each alumnus's consent. The public site is static on GitHub Pages:\nanything published is readable by anyone, including scrapers. The manual's content\ntypes are Posts and Pages, and neither has a field for consent. Options considered:\na public list with emails, obfuscated emails, names only with a contact path, and a\nMarkdown page typed by hand.\n\n## Decision\n\nAn alumnus's entry is a **Listing**, a third content type with name, email, optional\nclass year, consent date, and who recorded it. Only Listings with consent are\nexported, and only their **names** reach `site/`. Emails stay in the database. The\npublic Alumni Network page carries one Alumni Contact Line telling visitors to email\nthe Second Editor, who introduces them to the alumnus.\n\n## Consequences\n\n- The exporter never reads the email field, so a test can assert that no alumnus\n  email appears anywhere in `site/`.\n- Removing a Listing or withdrawing consent removes the name at the next publish,\n  with no leftover text in a page body.\n- An extra model, admin screen, and exporter step are needed beyond Posts and Pages.\n- Visitors cannot contact an alumnus directly. The Second Editor is a manual go-between.\n- Once names are published to GitHub Pages they may be cached or indexed, so\n  withdrawal cannot recall a name already published.\n\n## Why this is an ADR\n\nIt is hard to reverse (the model and the exporter both depend on it), surprising\nwithout context (the manual lists only Posts and Pages), and the result of a real\ntrade-off between findability and the privacy the client asked for.\n"}},"serverClassifierRequest":"bd27bd82-a9c1-4fbd-a662-464fd029cbda","apiBlockIndex":1,"requestId":"req_011Cfi9TSJD4y7AumzvinaqQ","type":"assistant","uuid":"6c67b517-5c58-4e04-ba6b-4253b9c7a3a6","timestamp":"2026-10-05T00:30:45.327Z","effort":"medium","perTurnEffort":"medium","session_id":"9d3ee875-b856-4c5f-8263-d5e667d38523","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/sphod/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"d6bafc9f-93fa-4ed5-bad2-bdfd45ad6274","version":"2.1.284","gitBranch":"main"}

It appears on Alumni Events.\n\n**Team Update**: A post with news about the current team, aimed at fans, parents, students and alumni.","new_string":"**Post Type**: The required choice on every post: *Event* or *Team Update*. It decides where the post appears.\n\n**Event**: A post of type Event, such as a reunion or game-watch. Date and place go in the title and body. It appears on Alumni Events and the Home page.\n\n**Team Update**: A post of type Team Update, with news about the current team for fans, parents, students and alumni. It appears on the Home page.\n\n**Home**: The front page. A welcome text, the latest three Team Updates, the most recent Events, and links to Team Schedule, Alumni Events and Alumni Network. Both editors can change it.\n\n**Deactivate**: Turn off a user's account so they cannot log in. Users are never deleted, so authors and \"recorded by\" always point at a real person. The Head Coach cannot deactivate or demote himself.","originalFile":"# CONTEXT.md glossary\n\nClient: Kenyon College Varsity Baseball Team Alumni.\n\n**Head Coach (Matt Burdette)**: The site's owner. Holds the *admin* role and can edit everything, including the Team Schedule.\n\n**Second Editor (Joe)**: Holds the *editor* role. Can write and publish Alumni Events and Alumni Network content, but can never edit the Team Schedule.\n\n**Team Schedule**: The current team's schedule, a single page in the public navigation. Only the Head Coach can edit it.\n\n**Alumni Events**: The public section for alumni gatherings. It is a page in the navigation that lists individual Event posts.\n\n**Event**: A single announcement (for example a reunion or game-watch) written as a post. Alumni cannot log in, so the Second Editor records the consent when the alumnus asks to be listed.\n\n**Consent date**: When an alumnus asked to be listed. Stored on the Listing.\n\n**Alumnus**: A former player on the varsity team. Never a user of the admin console.\n\n**Locked**: A page only the Head Coach may change. The Team Schedule is Locked. The Second Editor can open a Locked page to read it, but cannot edit, publish, unpublish or delete it. Only the Head Coach can lock or unlock a page.\n\n**Alumni Contact Line**: The single public line on the Alumni Network page telling visitors to email the Second Editor, who then introduces them to the alumnus. It is the only way to reach an alumnus through the site.\n\n**Publish**: The explicit step that turns published content into the public site. {"parentUuid":"a26be8f1-04e0-4241-b167-c3e73469cb94","isSidechain":false,"message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-5-5","id":"msg_011Cfi9U5EThGSo2yU4hYUnP","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"I wrote `docs/adr/ADR-003-alumni-listings-emails-never-published.md` and updated `CONTEXT.md`. The glossary now has 15 terms, including Post Type, Home and Deactivate.\n\nI checked what is still open and found nothing that blocks the spec. Here is every decision, so nothing is assumed silently.\n\n**Who and roles**\n- **Matt** is the admin. He can edit everything, including the Team Schedule, and he manages users.\n- **Joe** is the editor. He can edit and publish everything except Locked pages, and he has no access to user management.\n- **Locked pages:** the Team Schedule is Locked. Joe can open it but can't edit, publish, unpublish or delete it. Only Matt can lock or unlock a page.\n- **Deactivate only:** users are never deleted, and Matt can't deactivate or demote himself.\n\n**Content**\n- **Pages:** Home, Team Schedule, Alumni Events, Alumni Network and About appear in the nav.\n- **Posts:** each has a Type of Event or Team Update. Events appear on Alumni Events and Home, and Team Updates appear on Home. Dates and places go in the title and body.\n- **Team Schedule:** it's a hand-edited Markdown table.\n- **Listings:** a third content type with name, email, optional class year, consent date and recorded-by. Joe records consent when an alumnus asks to be listed.\n- **Alumni Network page:** an intro with the Alumni Contact Line, then an alphabetical list of consented names. Emails are never published, and visitors are told to email Joe to reach someone.\n\n**Operation and scope**\n- **Publishing:** you run `cms publish` as Matt's laptop in the demo. Matt is the intended operator in real life, and edits aren't live until publish runs.\n- **Out of scope:**\n  - alumni accounts or logins\n  - comments\n  - media uploads\n  - a public sign-up form\n  - a hosted admin\n  - RSVPs\n  - scheduled or recurring events\n  - a structured game and results database\n  - name search\n\n**Decisions on record:** ADR-001 and ADR-002 came with the assignment, and ADR-003 is the new one.\n\nOne thing to be aware of: the manual's rubric lists seven capabilities, and Listings sit beside them as an extension. I'd map them to capability 3 (Posts) for the CRUD pattern and capability 7 (publish) for the export. The spec's \"7 capabilities in your words\" section should say so plainly, so a grader doesn't read Listings as drift.\n\nDo you agree that we've reached a shared understanding? If you do, I'll leave `.claude/state/phase` as `grill`. You can then run `/to-spec` in this same window, as the manual says, and `/to-tickets` after it. I'd also suggest saving this session's transcript to `docs/transcripts/` before you clear."}],"container":null,"stop_reason":"end_turn","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1470,"cache_read_input_tokens":78204,"output_tokens":956,"output_tokens_details":{"thinking_tokens":0},"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":1470,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0},"inference_geo":"not_available","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":956,"cache_read_input_tokens":78204,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1470,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":1470},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"serverClassifierRequest":"9b818ac7-f373-4235-9ade-1e373fe38150","apiBlockIndex":0,"requestId":"req_011Cfi9U4w6Qo9ScYZqbcdfB","type":"assistant","uuid":"934da51d-4172-45d9-9e1a-6bef3d5ee5a7","timestamp":"2026-10-05T00:30:55.033Z","effort":"medium","perTurnEffort":"medium","session_id":"9d3ee875-b856-4c5f-8263-d5e667d38523","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/sphod/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"d6bafc9f-93fa-4ed5-bad2-bdfd45ad6274","version":"2.1.284","gitBranch":"main"}

(file state is current in your context — no need to Read it back)"}]},"uuid":"206d70af-ce5b-4ea4-bdd0-9d783ec497af","timestamp":"2026-10-05T00:33:40.594Z","toolUseResult":{"filePath":"/home/sphod/code/iphs400-mp2-cms/CONTEXT.md","oldString":"Can write and publish Alumni Events and Alumni Network content, but can never edit the Team Schedule.","newString":"Can write and publish any content, including Alumni Events and the Alumni Network, except Locked pages such as the Team Schedule. Cannot manage users.","originalFile":"# CONTEXT.md glossary\n\nClient: Kenyon College Varsity Baseball Team Alumni.\n\n**Head Coach (Matt Burdette)**: The site's owner. Holds the *admin* role and can edit everything, including the Team Schedule.\n\n**Second Editor (Joe)**: Holds the *editor* role. Can write and publish Alumni Events and Alumni Network content, but can never edit the Team Schedule.\n\n**Team Schedule**: The current team's schedule, a single page in the public navigation. Only the Head Coach can edit it.\n\n**Alumni Events**: The public section for alumni gatherings. It is a page in the navigation that lists individual Event posts.\n\n**Post Type**: The required choice on every post: *Event* or *Team Update*. It decides where the post appears.\n\n**Event**: A post of type Event, such as a reunion or game-watch. Date and place go in the title and body. It appears on Alumni Events and the Home page.\n\n**Team Update**: A post of type Team Update, with news about the current team for fans, parents, students and alumni. It appears on the Home page.\n\n**Home**: The front page. A welcome text, the latest three Team Updates, the most recent Events, and links to Team Schedule, Alumni Events and Alumni Network. Both editors can change it.\n\n**Deactivate**: Turn off a user's account so they cannot log in. Users are never deleted, so authors and \"recorded by\" always point at a real person. The Head Coach cannot deactivate or demote himself.\n\n**Alumni Network**: The public section listing alumni who have chosen to be listed. Shows names only; emails are never published.\n\n**Listing**: One alumnus's entry in the Alumni Network. Holds name, email, optional class year, consent date, and who recorded it. Only listings with consent reach the public site.\n\n**Opt-in**: An alumnus agrees to be listed. Alumni cannot log in, so the Second Editor records the consent when the alumnus asks to be listed.\n\n**Consent date**: When an alumnus asked to be listed. Stored on the Listing.\n\n**Alumnus**: A former player on the varsity team. Never a user of the admin console.\n\n**Locked**: A page only the Head Coach may change. The Team Schedule is Locked. The Second Editor can open a Locked page to read it, but cannot edit, publish, unpublish or delete it. Only the Head Coach can lock or unlock a page.\n\n**Alumni Contact Line**: The single public line on the Alumni Network page telling visitors to email the Second Editor, who then introduces them to the alumnus. It is the only way to reach an alumnus through the site.\n\n**Publish**: The explicit step that turns published content into the public site. Edits are not live until the Head Coach (or the builder, in the demo) runs it.\n","structuredPatch":[{"oldStart":4,"oldLines":7,"newStart":4,"newLines":7,"lines":[" "," **Head Coach (Matt Burdette)**: The site's owner. Holds the *admin* role and can edit everything, including the Team Schedule."," ","-**Second Editor (Joe)**: Holds the *editor* role. Can write and publish Alumni Events and Alumni Network content, but can never edit the Team Schedule.","+**Second Editor (Joe)**: Holds the *editor* role. Can write and publish any content, including Alumni Events and the Alumni Network, except Locked pages such as the Team Schedule. Cannot manage users."," "," **Team Schedule**: The current team's schedule, a single page in the public navigation. Only the Head Coach can edit it."," "]}],"userModified":false,"replaceAll":false},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"5bf19e6f-aacc-4d2d-9a24-c00e88ad0efb","session_id":"9d3ee875-b856-4c5f-8263-d5e667d38523","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/sphod/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"d6bafc9f-93fa-4ed5-bad2-bdfd45ad6274","version":"2.1.284","gitBranch":"main"}

{"parentUuid":"aa81cc34-1c73-4cc9-8e2f-1c8b725c1900","isSidechain":false,"message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-5-5","id":"msg_011Cfi9oebgmQxybUJH4b4TC","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"You don't need to close it. The clash comes from the `code` command: in WSL it launches the Windows VS Code, which refuses to start next to an instance running as administrator. Closing the first window would only help if you then reopened VS Code without admin rights.\n\nThe simplest fix is to open the file from the window you already have:\n\n- **If that window is connected to this repo** (the bottom-left corner says \"WSL: Ubuntu\" or similar), press `Ctrl+P`, type `CONTEXT.md` and press Enter.\n- **If it isn't**, use File → Open File and paste `\\\\wsl$\\Ubuntu\\home\\sphod\\code\\iphs400-mp2-cms\\CONTEXT.md`. Replace `Ubuntu` with your distro's name if it differs. You can also run `wsl.exe -l` in PowerShell to see it.\n\nThen you can close the stray second window. If you edit `CONTEXT.md` in VS Code, tell me before you run `/to-spec`, so I re-read it instead of working from my copy."}],"container":null,"stop_reason":"end_turn","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":68,"cache_read_input_tokens":82850,"output_tokens":475,"output_tokens_details":{"thinking_tokens":137},"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":68,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0},"inference_geo":"not_available","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":475,"cache_read_input_tokens":82850,"cache_creation_input_tokens":68,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":68},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"serverClassifierRequest":"d31979af-6e49-45dd-b895-5eb550653ad8","apiBlockIndex":1,"requestId":"req_011Cfi9oeCP2cS84FCQJDxkZ","type":"assistant","uuid":"8ae5871e-4be5-4125-9bf9-8fdd5cfc9321","timestamp":"2026-10-05T00:35:17.821Z","effort":"medium","perTurnEffort":"medium","session_id":"9d3ee875-b856-4c5f-8263-d5e667d38523","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/sphod/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"d6bafc9f-93fa-4ed5-bad2-bdfd45ad6274","version":"2.1.284","gitBranch":"main"}

It should post one GitHub issue labeled `spec`.\n\nWhen it posts, read the issue on GitHub before running `/to-tickets`. {"parentUuid":"1a53c52c-7cde-4847-ae79-20e025d94171","isSidechain":false,"promptId":"f7672b10-2484-4852-a837-faa8975be155","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Base directory for this skill: /home/sphod/.claude/plugins/cache/claude-plugins-official/mattpocock-skills/1.2.3/skills/engineering/to-spec\n\nThis skill takes the current conversation context and codebase understanding and produces a spec. Do NOT interview the user; just synthesize what you already know.\n\nThe issue tracker and triage label vocabulary should have been provided to you. If not, tell the user to run `/setup-matt-pocock-skills`.\n\n## Process\n\n1. Explore the repo to understand the current state of the codebase, if you haven't already. Use the project's domain glossary vocabulary throughout the spec, and respect any ADRs in the area you're touching.\n\n2. Sketch out the seams at which you're going to test the feature. Existing seams should be preferred to new ones. Use the highest seam possible. If new seams are needed, propose them at the highest point you can. The fewer seams across the codebase, the better - the ideal number is one.\n\nCheck with the user that these seams match their expectations.\n\n3. Write the spec using the template below, then publish it to the project issue tracker. Apply the `ready-for-agent` triage label - no need for additional triage.\n\n<spec-template>\n\n## Problem Statement\n\nThe problem that the user is facing, from the user's perspective.\n\n## Solution\n\nThe solution to the problem, from the user's perspective.\n\n## User Stories\n\nA LONG, numbered list of user stories. Each user story should be in the format of:\n\n1. As an <actor>, I want a <feature>, so that <benefit>\n\n<user-story-example>\n1. As a mobile bank customer, I want to see balance on my accounts, so that I can make better informed decisions about my spending\n</user-story-example>\n\nThis list of user stories should be extremely extensive and cover all aspects of the feature.\n\n## Implementation Decisions\n\nA list of implementation decisions that were made. This can include:\n\n- The modules that will be built/modified\n- The interfaces of those modules that will be modified\n- Technical clarifications from the developer\n- Architectural decisions\n- Schema changes\n- API contracts\n- Specific interactions\n\nDo NOT include specific file paths or code snippets. They may end up being outdated very quickly.\n\nException: if a prototype produced a snippet that encodes a decision more precisely than prose can (state machine, reducer, schema, type shape), inline it within the relevant decision and note briefly that it came from a prototype. Trim to the decision-rich parts, not a working demo, just the important bits.\n\n## Testing Decisions\n\nA list of testing decisions that were made. Include:\n\n- A description of what makes a good test (only test external behavior, not implementation details)\n- Which modules will be tested\n- Prior art for the tests (i.e. similar types of tests in the codebase)\n\n## Out of Scope\n\nA description of the things that are out of scope for this spec.\n\n## Further Notes\n\nAny further notes about the feature.\n\n</spec-template>\n"}]},"isMeta":true,"turnCompanion":true,"uuid":"fe9a838e-827b-447c-9c46-eea7b03cb083","timestamp":"2026-10-05T00:39:17.951Z","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/sphod/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"d6bafc9f-93fa-4ed5-bad2-bdfd45ad6274","version":"2.1.284","gitBranch":"main"}

find app templates tests scripts -type f -not -name '*.pyc' | head -60; cat pyproject.toml; sed -n 1,60p tests/conftest.py 2>/dev/null; gh label list 2>&1 | head -20","description":"Explore repo structure, tracker docs, and labels"}},"wireIngestContext":{"toolu_01NTpGmLJoJX1AJVwqRcnFwe":{"cwd":"/home/sphod/code/iphs400-mp2-cms"}},"serverClassifierRequest":"5d933fca-6cde-4ee1-85aa-a81119f3ab03","apiBlockIndex":1,"requestId":"req_011CfiA7gR53EeGLK3DxJYSm","attributionSkill":"mattpocock-skills:to-spec","attributionPlugin":"mattpocock-skills","type":"assistant","uuid":"80f99875-611f-423b-85c6-d649435b5057","timestamp":"2026-10-05T00:39:20.722Z","effort":"medium","perTurnEffort":"medium","session_id":"9d3ee875-b856-4c5f-8263-d5e667d38523","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/sphod/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"d6bafc9f-93fa-4ed5-bad2-bdfd45ad6274","version":"2.1.284","gitBranch":"main"}
{"parentUuid":"80f99875-611f-423b-85c6-d649435b5057","isSidechain":false,"promptId":"f7672b10-2484-4852-a837-faa8975be155","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01NTpGmLJoJX1AJVwqRcnFwe","type":"tool_result","content":"# Issue tracker: GitHub\n\nIssues and specs for this repo live as GitHub issues. Use the `gh` CLI for all operations.\n\n## Conventions\n\n- **Create an issue**: `gh issue create --title \"...\" --body \"...\"`. Use a heredoc for multi-line bodies.\n- **Read an issue**: `gh issue view <number> --comments`, filtering comments by `jq` and also fetching labels.\n- **List issues**: `gh issue list --state open --json number,title,body,labels,comments --jq '[.[] | {number, title, body, labels: [.labels[].name], comments: [.comments[].body]}]'` with appropriate `--label` and `--state` filters.\n- **Comment on an issue**: `gh issue comment <number> --body \"...\"`\n- **Apply / remove labels**: `gh issue edit <number> --add-label \"...\"` / `--remove-label \"...\"`\n- **Close**: `gh issue close <number> --comment \"...\"`\n\nInfer the repo from `git remote -v`; `gh` does this automatically when run inside a clone.\n\n## Pull requests as a triage surface\n\n**PRs as a request surface: no.** _(Set to `yes` if this repo treats external PRs as feature requests; `/triage` reads this flag.)_\n\nWhen set to `yes`, PRs run through the same labels and states as issues, using the `gh pr` equivalents:\n\n- **Read a PR**: `gh pr view <number> --comments` and `gh pr diff <number>` for the diff.\n- **List external PRs for triage**: `gh pr list --state open --json number,title,body,labels,author,authorAssociation,comments` then keep only `authorAssociation` of `CONTRIBUTOR`, `FIRST_TIME_CONTRIBUTOR`, or `NONE` (drop `OWNER`/`MEMBER`/`COLLABORATOR`).\n- **Comment / label / close**: `gh pr comment`, `gh pr edit --add-label`/`--remove-label`, `gh pr close`.\n\nGitHub shares one number space across issues and PRs, so a bare `#42` may be either: resolve with `gh pr view 42` and fall back to `gh issue view 42`.\n\n## When a skill says \"publish to the issue tracker\"\n\nCreate a GitHub issue.\n\n## When a skill says \"fetch the relevant ticket\"\n\nRun `gh issue view <number> --comments`.\n\n## Wayfinding operations\n\nUsed by `/wayfinder`. The **map** is a single issue with **child** issues as tickets.\n\n- **Map**: a single issue labelled `wayfinder:map`, holding the Notes / Decisions-so-far / Fog body. `gh issue create --label wayfinder:map`.\n- **Child ticket**: an issue linked to the map as a GitHub sub-issue (`gh api` on the sub-issues endpoint). Where sub-issues aren't enabled, add the child to a task list in the map body and put `Part of #<map>` at the top of the child body. Labels: `wayfinder:<type>` (`research`/`prototype`/`grilling`/`task`). Once claimed, the ticket is assigned to the driving dev.\n- **Blocking**: GitHub's **native issue dependencies**, the canonical, UI-visible representation. Add an edge with `gh api --method POST repos/<owner>/<repo>/issues/<child>/dependencies/blocked_by -F issue_id=<blocker-db-id>`, where `<blocker-db-id>` is the blocker's numeric **database id** (`gh api repos/<owner>/<repo>/issues/<n> --jq .id`, _not_ the `#number` or `node_id`). GitHub reports `issue_dependencies_summary.blocked_by` (open blockers only, the live gate). Where dependencies aren't available, fall back to a `Blocked by: #<n>, #<n>` line at the top of the child body. A ticket is unblocked when every blocker is closed.\n- **Frontier query**: list the map's open children (`gh issue list --state open`, scoped to the map's sub-issues / task list), drop any with an open blocker (`issue_dependencies_summary.blocked_by > 0`, or an open issue in the `Blocked by` line) or an assignee; first in map order wins.\n- **Claim**: `gh issue edit <n> --add-assignee @me`, the session's first write.\n- **Resolve**: `gh issue comment <n> --body \"<answer>\"`, then `gh issue close <n>`, then append a context pointer (gist + link) to the map's Decisions-so-far.\n# Triage Labels\n\nThe skills speak in terms of five canonical triage roles. This file maps those roles to the actual label strings used in this repo's issue tracker.\n\n| Label in mattpocock/skills | Label in our tracker | Meaning                                  |\n| -------------------------- | -------------------- | ---------------------------------------- |\n| `needs-triage`             | `needs-triage`       | Maintainer needs to evaluate this issue  |\n| `needs-info`               | `needs-info`         | Waiting on reporter for more information |\n| `ready-for-agent`          | `ready-for-agent`    | Fully specified, ready for an AFK agent  |\n| `ready-for-human`          | `ready-for-human`    | Requires human implementation            |\n| `wontfix`                  | `wontfix`            | Will not be actioned                     |\n\nWhen a skill mentions a role (e.g. \"apply the AFK-ready triage label\"), use the corresponding label string from this table.\n\nEdit the right-hand column to match whatever vocabulary you actually use.\napp/settings.py\napp/main.py\napp/__init__.py\napp/cli.py\napp/publish.py\ntemplates/base.html\ntests/test_t00.py\ntests/test_ctx_guard.py\ntests/conftest.py\ntests/test_usage_report.py\nscripts/seed_demo.py\nscripts/check_submission.py\nscripts/usage_report.py\ntemplates/public/home.html\ntemplates/admin/hello.html\n[project]\nname = \"iphs400-mp2-cms\"\nversion = \"0.1.0\"\ndescription = \"IPHS 400 MP2 — a small web CMS with a local admin console and a static public site\"\nrequires-python = \">=3.12\"\ndependencies = [\n    \"fastapi>=0.115\",\n    \"uvicorn[standard]>=0.32\",\n    \"jinja2>=3.1\",\n    \"python-multipart>=0.0.12\",   # form posts\n    \"itsdangerous>=2.2\",          # signed session cookies\n    \"argon2-cffi>=23.1\",          # password hashing (never store plain passwords)\n    \"markdown-it-py>=3.0\",        # Markdown -> HTML\n    \"nh3>=0.2\",                   # HTML sanitizer (strips <script> etc.)\n    \"ghp-import>=2.1\",            # push site/ to the gh-pages branch\n]\n\n[dependency-groups]\ndev = [\"pytest>=8.3\", \"httpx>=0.28\"]\n\n[project.scripts]\ncms = \"app.cli:main\"\n\n[tool.pytest.ini_options]\ntestpaths = [\"tests\"]\n\n[build-system]\nrequires = [\"hatchling\"]\nbuild-backend = \"hatchling.build\"\n\n[tool.hatch.build.targets.wheel]\npackages = [\"app\"]\n\"\"\"Shared test fixtures.\n\n`client` gives you the app. `client_as(role)` gives you a client that is logged\nin as a seeded user of that role — it works as soon as your login route exists,\nso access-control tests stay one line:\n\n    def test_editor_cannot_manage_users(client_as):\n        assert client_as(\"editor\").get(\"/admin/users\").status_code in (302, 403)\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport pytest\nfrom fastapi.testclient import TestClient\n\nfrom app.main import create_app\n\n# Matches scripts/seed_demo.py. Passwords come from the environment there; in\n# tests they are fixed and meaningless.\nDEMO_USERS = {\n    \"admin\": {\"email\": \"admin@example.test\", \"password\": \"test-admin-pw\"},\n    \"editor\": {\"email\": \"editor@example.test\", \"password\": \"test-editor-pw\"},\n}\n\n\n@pytest.fixture\ndef client() -> TestClient:\n    return TestClient(create_app())\n\n\n@pytest.fixture\ndef client_as():\n    \"\"\"Return a factory: client_as(\"editor\") -> a logged-in TestClient.\"\"\"\n\n    def _login(role: str) -> TestClient:\n        user = DEMO_USERS[role]\n        c = TestClient(create_app())\n        response = c.post(\"/login\", data={\"email\": user[\"email\"],\n                                          \"password\": user[\"password\"]},\n                          follow_redirects=False)\n        if response.status_code == 404:\n            pytest.skip(\"No /login route yet — build the login ticket first.\")\n        assert response.status_code in (200, 302, 303), (\n            f\"Login as {role} failed with {response.status_code}\")\n        return c\n\n    return _login\naccessibility\tBarrier affecting people with disabilities\t#f143ab\nbug\tSomething isn't working\t#d73a4a\ndocumentation\tImprovements or additions to documentation\t#0075ca\nduplicate\tThis issue or pull request already exists\t#cfd3d7\nenhancement\tNew feature or request\t#a2eeef\ngood first issue\tGood for newcomers\t#7057ff\nhelp wanted\tExtra attention is needed\t#008672\ninvalid\tThis doesn't seem right\t#e4e669\nquestion\tFurther information is requested\t#d876e3\nwontfix\tThis will not be worked on\t#ffffff","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"7b9f2f44-7e1c-414a-a674-a543196fcfa9","timestamp":"2026-10-05T00:39:22.530Z","toolUseResult":{"stdout":"# Issue tracker: GitHub\n\nIssues and specs for this repo live as GitHub issues. Use the `gh` CLI for all operations.\n\n## Conventions\n\n- **Create an issue**: `gh issue create --title \"...\" --body \"...\"`. Use a heredoc for multi-line bodies.\n- **Read an issue**: `gh issue view <number> --comments`, filtering comments by `jq` and also fetching labels.\n- **List issues**: `gh issue list --state open --json number,title,body,labels,comments --jq '[.[] | {number, title, body, labels: [.labels[].name], comments: [.comments[].body]}]'` with appropriate `--label` and `--state` filters.\n- **Comment on an issue**: `gh issue comment <number> --body \"...\"`\n- **Apply / remove labels**: `gh issue edit <number> --add-label \"...\"` / `--remove-label \"...\"`\n- **Close**: `gh issue close <number> --comment \"...\"`\n\nInfer the repo from `git remote -v`; `gh` does this automatically when run inside a clone.\n\n## Pull requests as a triage surface\n\n**PRs as a request surface: no.** _(Set to `yes` if this repo treats external PRs as feature requests; `/triage` reads this flag.)_\n\nWhen set to `yes`, PRs run through the same labels and states as issues, using the `gh pr` equivalents:\n\n- **Read a PR**: `gh pr view <number> --comments` and `gh pr diff <number>` for the diff.\n- **List external PRs for triage**: `gh pr list --state open --json number,title,body,labels,author,authorAssociation,comments` then keep only `authorAssociation` of `CONTRIBUTOR`, `FIRST_TIME_CONTRIBUTOR`, or `NONE` (drop `OWNER`/`MEMBER`/`COLLABORATOR`).\n- **Comment / label / close**: `gh pr comment`, `gh pr edit --add-label`/`--remove-label`, `gh pr close`.\n\nGitHub shares one number space across issues and PRs, so a bare `#42` may be either: resolve with `gh pr view 42` and fall back to `gh issue view 42`.\n\n## When a skill says \"publish to the issue tracker\"\n\nCreate a GitHub issue.\n\n## When a skill says \"fetch the relevant ticket\"\n\nRun `gh issue view <number> --comments`.\n\n## Wayfinding operations\n\nUsed by `/wayfinder`. The **map** is a single issue with **child** issues as tickets.\n\n- **Map**: a single issue labelled `wayfinder:map`, holding the Notes / Decisions-so-far / Fog body. `gh issue create --label wayfinder:map`.\n- **Child ticket**: an issue linked to the map as a GitHub sub-issue (`gh api` on the sub-issues endpoint). Where sub-issues aren't enabled, add the child to a task list in the map body and put `Part of #<map>` at the top of the child body. Labels: `wayfinder:<type>` (`research`/`prototype`/`grilling`/`task`). Once claimed, the ticket is assigned to the driving dev.\n- **Blocking**: GitHub's **native issue dependencies**, the canonical, UI-visible representation. Add an edge with `gh api --method POST repos/<owner>/<repo>/issues/<child>/dependencies/blocked_by -F issue_id=<blocker-db-id>`, where `<blocker-db-id>` is the blocker's numeric **database id** (`gh api repos/<owner>/<repo>/issues/<n> --jq .id`, _not_ the `#number` or `node_id`). GitHub reports `issue_dependencies_summary.blocked_by` (open blockers only, the live gate). Where dependencies aren't available, fall back to a `Blocked by: #<n>, #<n>` line at the top of the child body. A ticket is unblocked when every blocker is closed.\n- **Frontier query**: list the map's open children (`gh issue list --state open`, scoped to the map's sub-issues / task list), drop any with an open blocker (`issue_dependencies_summary.blocked_by > 0`, or an open issue in the `Blocked by` line) or an assignee; first in map order wins.\n- **Claim**: `gh issue edit <n> --add-assignee @me`, the session's first write.\n- **Resolve**: `gh issue comment <n> --body \"<answer>\"`, then `gh issue close <n>`, then append a context pointer (gist + link) to the map's Decisions-so-far.\n# Triage Labels\n\nThe skills speak in terms of five canonical triage roles. This file maps those roles to the actual label strings used in this repo's issue tracker.\n\n| Label in mattpocock/skills | Label in our tracker | Meaning                                  |\n| -------------------------- | -------------------- | ---------------------------------------- |\n| `needs-triage`             | `needs-triage`       | Maintainer needs to evaluate this issue  |\n| `needs-info`               | `needs-info`         | Waiting on reporter for more information |\n| `ready-for-agent`          | `ready-for-agent`    | Fully specified, ready for an AFK agent  |\n| `ready-for-human`          | `ready-for-human`    | Requires human implementation            |\n| `wontfix`                  | `wontfix`            | Will not be actioned                     |\n\nWhen a skill mentions a role (e.g. \"apply the AFK-ready triage label\"), use the corresponding label string from this table.\n\nEdit the right-hand column to match whatever vocabulary you actually use.\napp/settings.py\napp/main.py\napp/__init__.py\napp/cli.py\napp/publish.py\ntemplates/base.html\ntests/test_t00.py\ntests/test_ctx_guard.py\ntests/conftest.py\ntests/test_usage_report.py\nscripts/seed_demo.py\nscripts/check_submission.py\nscripts/usage_report.py\ntemplates/public/home.html\ntemplates/admin/hello.html\n[project]\nname = \"iphs400-mp2-cms\"\nversion = \"0.1.0\"\ndescription = \"IPHS 400 MP2 — a small web CMS with a local admin console and a static public site\"\nrequires-python = \">=3.12\"\ndependencies = [\n    \"fastapi>=0.115\",\n    \"uvicorn[standard]>=0.32\",\n    \"jinja2>=3.1\",\n    \"python-multipart>=0.0.12\",   # form posts\n    \"itsdangerous>=2.2\",          # signed session cookies\n    \"argon2-cffi>=23.1\",          # password hashing (never store plain passwords)\n    \"markdown-it-py>=3.0\",        # Markdown -> HTML\n    \"nh3>=0.2\",                   # HTML sanitizer (strips <script> etc.)\n    \"ghp-import>=2.1\",            # push site/ to the gh-pages branch\n]\n\n[dependency-groups]\ndev = [\"pytest>=8.3\", \"httpx>=0.28\"]\n\n[project.scripts]\ncms = \"app.cli:main\"\n\n[tool.pytest.ini_options]\ntestpaths = [\"tests\"]\n\n[build-system]\nrequires = [\"hatchling\"]\nbuild-backend = \"hatchling.build\"\n\n[tool.hatch.build.targets.wheel]\npackages = [\"app\"]\n\"\"\"Shared test fixtures.\n\n`client` gives you the app. `client_as(role)` gives you a client that is logged\nin as a seeded user of that role — it works as soon as your login route exists,\nso access-control tests stay one line:\n\n    def test_editor_cannot_manage_users(client_as):\n        assert client_as(\"editor\").get(\"/admin/users\").status_code in (302, 403)\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport pytest\nfrom fastapi.testclient import TestClient\n\nfrom app.main import create_app\n\n# Matches scripts/seed_demo.py. Passwords come from the environment there; in\n# tests they are fixed and meaningless.\nDEMO_USERS = {\n    \"admin\": {\"email\": \"admin@example.test\", \"password\": \"test-admin-pw\"},\n    \"editor\": {\"email\": \"editor@example.test\", \"password\": \"test-editor-pw\"},\n}\n\n\n@pytest.fixture\ndef client() -> TestClient:\n    return TestClient(create_app())\n\n\n@pytest.fixture\ndef client_as():\n    \"\"\"Return a factory: client_as(\"editor\") -> a logged-in TestClient.\"\"\"\n\n    def _login(role: str) -> TestClient:\n        user = DEMO_USERS[role]\n        c = TestClient(create_app())\n        response = c.post(\"/login\", data={\"email\": user[\"email\"],\n                                          \"password\": user[\"password\"]},\n                          follow_redirects=False)\n        if response.status_code == 404:\n            pytest.skip(\"No /login route yet — build the login ticket first.\")\n        assert response.status_code in (200, 302, 303), (\n            f\"Login as {role} failed with {response.status_code}\")\n        return c\n\n    return _login\naccessibility\tBarrier affecting people with disabilities\t#f143ab\nbug\tSomething {"parentUuid":"ab7452b2-3f87-41a3-9dbb-16c32b24df37","isSidechain":false,"message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-5-5","id":"msg_011CfiAQQMfAesAU7gqGfuX8","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The last turn stopped after exploring the repo. It's still missing a seams check, which the skill requires before it publishes.\n\n**What the repo has now:** only the T00 skeleton. That's `create_app()` in `app/main.py`, `app/publish.py` and the `cms` CLI, with a `client` and a `client_as(role)` fixture in `tests/conftest.py`. I also found that the GitHub repo has no `spec`, `ticket` or `ready-for-agent` labels yet. I'll create them as part of publishing.\n\n**Proposed test seams, with as few as I can manage:**\n\n1. **HTTP seam, which gets most of the tests.** Drive `create_app()` through the existing `client` and `client_as(\"admin\" | \"editor\")` fixtures. It covers login and logout, roles, Locked pages, Post and Page CRUD, Listing CRUD, user management and deactivation, CSRF rejection, the filterable content list, and the Markdown preview.\n2. **Publish seam, with one new test.** Run the export into a temp directory and assert on the files in `site/`:\n   - Drafts and unconsented Listings are absent.\n   - No email address appears anywhere.\n   - Every path is relative.\n   - A `<script>` tag is stripped.\n   - The nav is built from published pages.\n   - The Home page, Alumni Events and the Alumni Network list are generated correctly.\n\nThat's two seams, and the publish one has to be separate because its output is files, not HTTP responses. I'd add no unit-level seams. Password hashing and slug rules are checked through the HTTP seam, for example by reading the stored hash after login.\n\nDo these seams match what you expect? {"parentUuid":"697aabc8-fa61-4169-bf75-937a747c8d3e","isSidechain":false,"message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-5-5","id":"msg_011CfiAV86FW64soZaXA28xg","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Checking the seams against the manual and rubric, I found four gaps and will fold them into the spec's testing section:\n\n1. **Restart persistence (rubric C3).** Post and Page CRUD must survive a server restart. The HTTP seam will build a fresh app on the same database file and check that the content is still there.\n2. **Access-control coverage (D3).** The tests will enumerate the admin-only routes from the app's own router, so a route added later can't escape the \"editor gets 403 or redirect, anonymous gets redirected to login\" check.\n3. **CSRF per form (D2).** The tests will replay each state-changing POST without a token, one per form, matching how the judge replays them.\n4. **Publish checks (F2, D5, E4).** The publish seam also needs a link check (every internal link resolves, no `href=\"/` or `src=\"/`) and a test that the demo seed script runs clean from `.env.example` values.\n\nThe rest already fits: argon2 hashes are read from the database (D1), and sanitization is checked in both the admin preview and the export (D4).\n\nNow I'll create the labels and write the spec."}],"container":null,"stop_reason":"tool_use","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":59,"cache_read_input_tokens":90205,"output_tokens":5,"output_tokens_details":{"thinking_tokens":0},"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":59,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0},"inference_geo":"not_available","iterations":[],"speed":"standard"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"serverClassifierRequest":"4c75abc3-fa91-4519-943d-b6ee2df23aa8","apiBlockIndex":1,"requestId":"req_011CfiAV7iBieq53tQGqT6ek","type":"assistant","uuid":"9401f6e4-76d0-4738-afb7-5509fc602d12","timestamp":"2026-10-05T00:44:20.191Z","effort":"medium","perTurnEffort":"medium","session_id":"9d3ee875-b856-4c5f-8263-d5e667d38523","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/sphod/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"d6bafc9f-93fa-4ed5-bad2-bdfd45ad6274","version":"2.1.284","gitBranch":"main"}

{"parentUuid":"b0a923d7-933f-4af7-9a04-e2293ef2b018","isSidechain":false,"message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-5-5","id":"msg_011CfiAkyzXriKCxkHL7UJ1o","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"","signature":"CAQSjwUKEAgSGAI4AUIIdGhpbmtpbmcSDC9Ks0iJ6Qb3FYCuBRoMdSc7t/IpFCoslMeLIjCejmRq442iMJTTaQK4ZBMwL3ly+UopAQLy5HpYGxWNZxOWBPjeuPGHdpE2o85oho4qrAQTw2PRoQ9gnGD6pUY2IOILVerE4J9cOuwqtAgRxW2MBb51KIZAsJG/cys8rLBGtHw4fLdignsOQam8GVKvVJIjFpr9i0Zfn5zv8AzbxrojI9bBvRFG5thWTVE3Tj3lhwuIx8uvrl3PfuP8TF0WuyF4/HWwVlQHcRHQiQqNkuSpasDaAbMX+CRwOielPznqWngLnIaupMBG2wMYjqiDZjIE2SPkPjlfZS8p2mQ9jxz7no654Mm/b3wkJJ0d9WsW8ezQvc0yXvFUd5gfFl4HytFXzeI/80fWAUCet3Q+4wETvNFwIKCgHUA30T1BYjKe+t05WviM3KLRx+9CSOE+juhjzC2PxRp+eqys6Ksoj6Oz8AxuHkhxAP5rJ0o1cYskYRjFWWTwR8GJuhtqVVMI5wzpY8C96rTfcuQcjAk85HOl3RAsqaykFrZSRjJk/QoYMK5kJqwhwNEwXYIK55X/aW0+QVuPpukl10zSri6v/N2GfmZJJ4NjIprBwQWNR8NnEyokZYC9HAXcCHJMnbv+5hjDq2dYcuf8cc3D7ia447OqygBD+p5T/AwvrNypufyr9tHOIoeR9e+Hh06ibyJW8dvaKHbunP1DSETe3N9/us37wdULi3Q/+BvEp73nFdFQp6ddYpCcD2cZY6QW/2INcGV13jzF5PmjE1AG3hDcciH5DfVUBI7ve0C6fBVT1QHnubZLG1lAzWbk0MuRPbPNxeBq5keeJNss/v1+mhI8GAE="}],"container":null,"stop_reason":"tool_use","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":336,"cache_read_input_tokens":91912,"output_tokens":5170,"output_tokens_details":{"thinking_tokens":19},"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":336,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0},"inference_geo":"not_available","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":5170,"cache_read_input_tokens":91912,"cache_creation_input_tokens":336,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":336},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"serverClassifierRequest":"4b024955-7773-4c5e-b5a8-6260d4a84dbd","apiBlockIndex":0,"requestId":"req_011CfiAkyaEcnnJZPipJuCfR","type":"assistant","uuid":"ee267d4b-4fac-4bd6-bac0-a0bdc6f4a89f","timestamp":"2026-10-05T00:47:45.355Z","effort":"medium","perTurnEffort":"medium","session_id":"9d3ee875-b856-4c5f-8263-d5e667d38523","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/sphod/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"d6bafc9f-93fa-4ed5-bad2-bdfd45ad6274","version":"2.1.284","gitBranch":"main"}
{"parentUuid":"ee267d4b-4fac-4bd6-bac0-a0bdc6f4a89f","isSidechain":false,"message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-5-5","id":"msg_011CfiAkyzXriKCxkHL7UJ1o","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01NDCiLZvBV3kpk1BK9ZFUZt","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/tmp/claude-1000/-home-sphod-code-iphs400-mp2-cms/d6bafc9f-93fa-4ed5-bad2-bdfd45ad6274/scratchpad/spec.md","content":"## Problem Statement\n\nKenyon College Varsity Baseball Team Alumni have no website of their own. Alumni, players, fans, students and parents learn about the team, its schedule and alumni gatherings only by talking to each other. The site is run by the Head Coach (Matt Burdette), who is comfortable using websites but cannot build or edit one, and by a Second Editor (Joe), who looks after the alumni side. Matt must be able to change anything. Joe must never be able to change the Team Schedule. Nobody else may ever edit the site. Alumni who want to be listed must be able to choose it, and their emails must not be exposed to the public internet.\n\n## Solution\n\nA small CMS in two parts, as set by ADR-001 and ADR-002. A private admin console, run on Matt's laptop (the builder's laptop in the demo), lets Matt and Joe log in and manage pages, posts, alumni listings and users. A `cms publish` step renders only the published content into plain, relative-path HTML in `site/`, which is deployed to GitHub Pages.\n\nThe public site has these pages in its navigation: **Home**, **Team Schedule**, **Alumni Events** and **Alumni Network**, plus **About**. Events and Team Updates are posts. Alumni Network shows consented alumni names only, and tells visitors to email Joe to reach an alumnus (ADR-003).\n\n### The 7 required capabilities, in this client's words\n\n1. **Login** - Matt and Joe sign in and out. Passwords are stored as argon2 hashes. A wrong password is refused with a message.\n2. **Two roles** - *admin* (Matt) can do everything. *editor* (Joe) can write and publish any content except **Locked** pages, and cannot manage users.\n3. **Posts** - Events and Team Updates. Create, read, update and delete, with title, slug, Markdown body, draft/published status, Post Type, author and timestamps.\n4. **Pages** - Home, Team Schedule, Alumni Events, Alumni Network, About and any others. Same fields and operations as posts, and a published page appears in the public navigation. The Team Schedule is Locked.\n5. **Admin console** - a dashboard with counts, a content list filterable by status and type, and an editor with a Markdown preview.\n6. **User management** - admin only: create users, change roles, deactivate and reactivate. A deactivated user cannot log in.\n7. **Public site and publish** - `cms publish` writes `site/` with published content only, sanitized Markdown, a navigation built from published pages, and relative paths.\n\n**Listings** are an extension beside these seven, not a replacement. They reuse the CRUD pattern of capability 3 and feed the export in capability 7. They exist because opt-in consent needs its own fields (see ADR-003).\n\n## User Stories\n\n1. As the Head Coach, I want to log in with my email and password, so that only I and Joe can change the site.\n2. As the Head Coach, I want a wrong password to be refused with a clear message, so that I know to try again.\n3. As the Head Coach, I want to log out, so that nobody else at a shared computer can use my session.\n4. As the Head Coach, I want my password stored only as a hash, so that it cannot be read from the database.\n5. As the Second Editor, I want to log in and out like Matt, so that my edits are attributed to me.\n6. As a visitor who is not logged in, I want to be sent to the login page when I try to open an admin address, so that I cannot see or change anything.\n7. As the Head Coach, I want to edit the Team Schedule, so that fans and alumni see the current schedule.\n8. As the Head Coach, I want the Team Schedule to be Locked, so that only I can change it.\n9. As the Second Editor, I want to open the Team Schedule and read it, so that I can see what the public sees.\n10. As the Second Editor, I want to be refused, with a clear message, when I try to edit, publish, unpublish or delete the Team Schedule, so that I know it is not mine to change.\n11. As the Head Coach, I want to lock or unlock any page, so that I can protect other pages if I need to.\n12. As the Second Editor, I want not to see lock or unlock controls, so that I cannot unlock the Team Schedule myself.\n13. As the Head Coach, I want to see counts of posts, pages, drafts and listings on a dashboard, so that I know the state of the site at a glance.\n14. As an editor, I want to filter the content list by status (draft or published) and by type, so that I can find what I need quickly.\n15. As an editor, I want to write a post in Markdown and see a preview before saving, so that I can check how it will look.\n16. As an editor, I want to choose a Post Type of Event or Team Update on every post, so that it appears in the right place.\n17. As the Second Editor, I want new posts to default to Event, so that my usual work needs one fewer click.\n18. As an editor, I want a slug generated from the title that I can change, so that I never have to invent a web address.\n19. As an editor, I want to save a post as a draft, so that I can finish it later without the public seeing it.\n20. As an editor, I want to publish and unpublish a post, so that I control what is public.\n21. As an editor, I want to delete a post, so that mistakes can be removed.\n22. As an editor, I want to see a success or error message after every save, so that I know it worked.\n23. As an editor, I want to see who wrote a post and when it was created and last updated, so that I can tell who changed what.\n24. As the Second Editor, I want to publish my Events and Alumni Network content without the Head Coach's approval, so that alumni news goes out promptly.\n25. As an editor, I want to create, edit, publish, unpublish and delete pages, so that the site's sections stay current.\n26. As a visitor, I want published pages to appear in the site's navigation, so that I can find each section.\n27. As an editor, I want an unpublished page to be missing from the navigation, so that unfinished sections are not linked.\n28. As the Second Editor, I want to add an alumnus as a Listing with name, email, optional class year and a consent date, so that only alumni who asked are on the list.\n29. As the Second Editor, I want each Listing to record who entered it and when the alumnus consented, so that there is a record of the opt-in.\n30. As the Second Editor, I want to remove a Listing or withdraw its consent, so that a name disappears at the next publish.\n31. As the Second Editor, I want a Listing without consent never to be published, so that nobody is listed by mistake.\n32. As an alumnus, I want my email never to appear on the public site, so that I am not exposed to spam.\n33. As a visitor, I want to see an alphabetical list of the alumni who chose to be listed, so that I can see who is in the network.\n34. As a visitor, I want a single line on the Alumni Network page telling me to email Joe to reach an alumnus, so that I know how to get in touch.\n35. As a visitor, I want the Alumni Events page to list Event posts newest first, so that I can see what is coming up.\n36. As a fan, I want the Home page to show a welcome, the latest three Team Updates and recent Events, so that I see news first.\n37. As a visitor, I want links from the Home page to Team Schedule, Alumni Events and Alumni Network, so that I can reach each section quickly.\n38. As a visitor on a phone, I want every public page to fit a narrow screen without sideways scrolling, so that I can read it anywhere.\n39. As the Head Coach, I want to create a user and give them the editor or admin role, so that I can add a helper.\n40. As the Head Coach, I want to change a user's role, so that I can adjust what they may do.\n41. As the Head Coach, I want to deactivate a user, so that a former helper can no longer log in.\n42. As the Head Coach, I want to reactivate a user, so that returning helpers get their access back.\n43. As the Head Coach, I want users never to be deleted, so that authors and \"recorded by\" always point to a real person.\n44. As the Head Coach, I want to be unable to deactivate or demote myself, so that the site can never be left with no admin.\n45. As the Second Editor, I want to be refused the Users screen, so that only the Head Coach manages accounts.\n46. As a deactivated user, I want to be refused at login with a message, so that I understand why.\n47. As the Head Coach, I want every form to carry a CSRF token, so that another website cannot change content through my session.\n48. As an editor, I want any `<script>` or `onerror=` in my Markdown stripped, in the preview and on the public site, so that nobody is attacked through the content.\n49. As the Head Coach, I want `cms publish` to write only published content, so that drafts never leave the database.\n50. As the Head Coach, I want published pages to use relative paths, so that the site works from the GitHub Pages subfolder.\n51. As the Head Coach, I want edits to stay private until I run publish, so that I choose when the public sees a change.\n52. As a grader, I want a seed script that fills the database with demo data from `.env.example` values, so that the app can be walked through from a clean clone.\n\n## Implementation Decisions\n\n- **Two seams.** Behaviour is exposed through the HTTP application and through the publish export. Nothing else is a public interface.\n- **Content model.** Three content types: Page, Post and Listing. Plus Users.\n  - Pages and Posts share title, slug, Markdown body, status (draft or published), author and timestamps.\n  - Posts add a required Post Type: Event or Team Update.\n  - Pages add a Locked flag, changeable only by an admin.\n  - Listings carry name, email, optional class year, consent date, and recorded-by.\n- **Roles.** Two roles: admin and editor. A single permission check guards every route.\n  - Editors cannot reach user management.\n  - Editors can open a Locked page but cannot edit, publish, unpublish or delete it.\n  - Anonymous visitors are redirected to login.\n- **Users.** Users are deactivated, never deleted. A deactivated user cannot log in. An admin cannot deactivate or demote themselves.\n- **Login and sessions.** Passwords hashed with argon2. Signed session cookies. The login form takes email and password. Secrets are read from the environment.\n- **CSRF.** Every state-changing form carries a token, and a POST without a valid token is rejected.\n- **Markdown.** Rendered then sanitized before it is shown anywhere, including the admin preview, per the CLAUDE.md hard constraints.\n- **Admin console.** A dashboard of counts, a content list filterable by status and type, an editor with a preview, a Listings screen and a Users screen. Persistent navigation and a success or error message after each save.\n- **Special pages.** The exporter gives the Home, Alumni Events and Alumni Network pages their generated sections: recent posts, the Event list, and the consented names. The page's own Markdown is rendered above them.\n- **Alumni data.** The exporter never reads the Listing email field (ADR-003). Only consented Listings are exported, names only, in alphabetical order.\n- **Publish.** `cms publish` renders published Pages, Posts and consented Listings into `site/` with relative paths only. `site/` is generated and never edited by hand. Drafts, unconsented Listings and emails are never exported.\n- **Deployment.** `cms deploy` pushes `site/` to the `gh-pages` branch. The admin console is never deployed (ADR-001).\n- **Demo data.** The seed script creates Matt as admin, Joe as editor, the five pages with Team Schedule Locked, sample posts of both types, and sample Listings.\n\n## Testing Decisions\n\n- **What makes a good test.** It asserts external behaviour only: an HTTP status, a redirect, a message, a file's content. It does not reach into internals.\n- **HTTP seam (most tests).** Use the existing `client` and `client_as(role)` fixtures and the app factory. Cover login and logout, roles, Locked pages, Post, Page and Listing CRUD, user management and deactivation, the content list filters, and the preview.\n  - **Persistence.** Post and Page CRUD must survive a restart: build a fresh app on the same database file and check the content is still there.\n  - **Access control.** Enumerate the admin-only routes from the app's own router, and assert an editor gets 403 or a redirect and an anonymous visitor is redirected to login, so a route added later cannot escape the check.\n  - **CSRF.** Replay each state-changing POST without a token and assert rejection, one per form.\n  - **Password hashing.** Read the stored hash and assert it is an argon2 hash, never plain text.\n- **Publish seam.** Run the export into a temporary directory and assert on the files:\n  - Drafts and unconsented Listings are absent.\n  - No alumnus email appears anywhere in the output.\n  - Every path is relative, with no `href=\"/` or `src=\"/`, and every internal link resolves.\n  - A `<script>` tag and an `onerror=` attribute are stripped.\n  - The navigation is built from published pages only, and Home, Alumni Events and the Alumni Network list are generated correctly.\n- **Seed script.** A test runs it from `.env.example` values and checks it completes with no manual steps.\n- **Prior art.** The existing walking-skeleton test and the `client_as` helper, whose docstring already shows the access-control pattern.\n- **Traceability.** Each ticket's acceptance criteria should be named by at least one test.\n\n## Out of Scope\n\n- Alumni accounts or logins, because the public site is static.\n- Comments.\n- Media uploads.\n- A public sign-up form for alumni.\n- A hosted admin console on the internet.\n- RSVPs, because a static site cannot take submissions.\n- Scheduled or recurring events.\n- A structured game and results database. The Team Schedule is a hand-edited Markdown table.\n- Search, including a name search on the Alumni Network.\n- Multi-site, drag-and-drop page builders and rich-text WYSIWYG editors, as the manual excludes them.\n\n## Further Notes\n\n- **Decisions made in the grill:** the content model and page names, Joe's role and Locked pages, consent and email privacy, the Alumni Contact Line, publish operator, home page, user rules, and ADR-003.\n- **Defaults not decided in the grill. Please confirm or correct:**\n  - Slugs are generated from the title and editable.\n  - Navigation shows Home first, then the other published pages alphabetically.\n  - Home shows three Team Updates and three recent Events.\n  - Home, Alumni Events and Alumni Network are recognised by fixed slugs for their generated sections.\n  - Joe's public contact address is a site setting, not part of a Listing.\n- **Real-world operation.** Matt is the intended operator of the admin console and `cms publish`. In the demo the builder's laptop plays Matt's. Published withdrawals of a name cannot recall a page already cached by GitHub Pages.\n- **Vocabulary.** Terms follow `CONTEXT.md`. ADR-001, ADR-002 and ADR-003 apply.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"container":null,"stop_reason":"tool_use","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":336,"cache_read_input_tokens":91912,"output_tokens":5170,"output_tokens_details":{"thinking_tokens":19},"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":336,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0},"inference_geo":"not_available","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":5170,"cache_read_input_tokens":91912,"cache_creation_input_tokens":336,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":336},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"wireToolInputs":{"toolu_01NDCiLZvBV3kpk1BK9ZFUZt":{"file_path":"/tmp/claude-1000/-home-sphod-code-iphs400-mp2-cms/d6bafc9f-93fa-4ed5-bad2-bdfd45ad6274/scratchpad/spec.md","content":"## Problem Statement\n\nKenyon College Varsity Baseball Team Alumni have no website of their own.
