As the Head Coach, I want to change a user's role, so that I can adjust what they may do.\n41. As the Head Coach, I want to deactivate a user, so that a former helper can no longer log in.\n42. As the Head Coach, I want to reactivate a user, so that returning helpers get their access back.\n43. As the Head Coach, I want users never to be deleted, so that authors and \"recorded by\" always point to a real person.\n44. As the Head Coach, I want to be unable to deactivate or demote myself, so that the site can never be left with no admin.\n45. As the Second Editor, I want to be refused the Users screen, so that only the Head Coach manages accounts.\n46. As a deactivated user, I want to be refused at login with a message, so that I understand why.\n47. As the Head Coach, I want every form to carry a CSRF token, so that another website cannot change content through my session.\n48. As an editor, I want any `<script>` or `onerror=` in my Markdown stripped, in the preview and on the public site, so that nobody is attacked through the content.\n49. As the Head Coach, I want `cms publish` to write only published content, so that drafts never leave the database.\n50. As the Head Coach, I want published pages to use relative paths, so that the site works from the GitHub Pages subfolder.\n51. As the Head Coach, I want edits to stay private until I run publish, so that I choose when the public sees a change.\n52. As a grader, I want a seed script that fills the database with demo data from `.env.example` values, so that the app can be walked through from a clean clone.\n\n## Implementation Decisions\n\n- **Two seams.** Behaviour is exposed through the HTTP application and through the publish export. Nothing else is a public interface.\n- **Content model.** Three content types: Page, Post and Listing. Plus Users.\n  - Pages and Posts share title, slug, Markdown body, status (draft or published), author and timestamps.\n  - Posts add a required Post Type: Event or Team Update.\n  - Pages add a Locked flag, changeable only by an admin.\n  - Listings carry name, email, optional class year, consent date, and recorded-by.\n- **Roles.** Two roles: admin and editor. A single permission check guards every route.\n  - Editors cannot reach user management.\n  - Editors can open a Locked page but cannot edit, publish, unpublish or delete it.\n  - Anonymous visitors are redirected to login.\n- **Users.** Users are deactivated, never deleted. A deactivated user cannot log in. An admin cannot deactivate or demote themselves.\n- **Login and sessions.** Passwords hashed with argon2. Signed session cookies. The login form takes email and password. Secrets are read from the environment.\n- **CSRF.** Every state-changing form carries a token, and a POST without a valid token is rejected.\n- **Markdown.** Rendered then sanitized before it is shown anywhere, including the admin preview, per the CLAUDE.md hard constraints.\n- **Admin console.** A dashboard of counts, a content list filterable by status and type, an editor with a preview, a Listings screen and a Users screen. Persistent navigation and a success or error message after each save.\n- **Special pages.** The exporter gives the Home, Alumni Events and Alumni Network pages their generated sections: recent posts, the Event list, and the consented names. The page's own Markdown is rendered above them.\n- **Alumni data.** The exporter never reads the Listing email field (ADR-003). Only consented Listings are exported, names only, in alphabetical order.\n- **Publish.** `cms publish` renders published Pages, Posts and consented Listings into `site/` with relative paths only. `site/` is generated and never edited by hand. Drafts, unconsented Listings and emails are never exported.\n- **Deployment.** `cms deploy` pushes `site/` to the `gh-pages` branch. The admin console is never deployed (ADR-001).\n- **Demo data.** The seed script creates Matt as admin, Joe as editor, the five pages with Team Schedule Locked, sample posts of both types, and sample Listings.\n\n## Testing Decisions\n\n- **What makes a good test.** It asserts external behaviour only: an HTTP status, a redirect, a message, a file's content. It does not reach into internals.\n- **HTTP seam (most tests).** Use the existing `client` and `client_as(role)` fixtures and the app factory. Cover login and logout, roles, Locked pages, Post, Page and Listing CRUD, user management and deactivation, the content list filters, and the preview.\n  - **Persistence.** Post and Page CRUD must survive a restart: build a fresh app on the same database file and check the content is still there.\n  - **Access control.** Enumerate the admin-only routes from the app's own router, and assert an editor gets 403 or a redirect and an anonymous visitor is redirected to login, so a route added later cannot escape the check.\n  - **CSRF.** Replay each state-changing POST without a token and assert rejection, one per form.\n  - **Password hashing.** Read the stored hash and assert it is an argon2 hash, never plain text.\n- **Publish seam.** Run the export into a temporary directory and assert on the files:\n  - Drafts and unconsented Listings are absent.\n  - No alumnus email appears anywhere in the output.\n  - Every path is relative, with no `href=\"/` or `src=\"/`, and every internal link resolves.\n  - A `<script>` tag and an `onerror=` attribute are stripped.\n  - The navigation is built from published pages only, and Home, Alumni Events and the Alumni Network list are generated correctly.\n- **Seed script.** A test runs it from `.env.example` values and checks it completes with no manual steps.\n- **Prior art.** The existing walking-skeleton test and the `client_as` helper, whose docstring already shows the access-control pattern.\n- **Traceability.** Each ticket's acceptance criteria should be named by at least one test.\n\n## Out of Scope\n\n- Alumni accounts or logins, because the public site is static.\n- Comments.\n- Media uploads.\n- A public sign-up form for alumni.\n- A hosted admin console on the internet.\n- RSVPs, because a static site cannot take submissions.\n- Scheduled or recurring events.\n- A structured game and results database. The Team Schedule is a hand-edited Markdown table.\n- Search, including a name search on the Alumni Network.\n- Multi-site, drag-and-drop page builders and rich-text WYSIWYG editors, as the manual excludes them.\n\n## Further Notes\n\n- **Decisions made in the grill:** the content model and page names, Joe's role and Locked pages, consent and email privacy, the Alumni Contact Line, publish operator, home page, user rules, and ADR-003.\n- **Defaults not decided in the grill. Please confirm or correct:**\n  - Slugs are generated from the title and editable.\n  - Navigation shows Home first, then the other published pages alphabetically.\n  - Home shows three Team Updates and three recent Events.\n  - Home, Alumni Events and Alumni Network are recognised by fixed slugs for their generated sections.\n  - Joe's public contact address is a site setting, not part of a Listing.\n- **Real-world operation.** Matt is the intended operator of the admin console and `cms publish`. In the demo the builder's laptop plays Matt's. Published withdrawals of a name cannot recall a page already cached by GitHub Pages.\n- **Vocabulary.** Terms follow `CONTEXT.md`. ADR-001, ADR-002 and ADR-003 apply.\n"}},"serverClassifierRequest":"4b024955-7773-4c5e-b5a8-6260d4a84dbd","apiBlockIndex":1,"requestId":"req_011CfiAkyaEcnnJZPipJuCfR","type":"assistant","uuid":"715752f6-a180-4cfc-b1d4-5cc471f278ba","timestamp":"2026-10-05T00:48:18.270Z","effort":"medium","perTurnEffort":"medium","session_id":"9d3ee875-b856-4c5f-8263-d5e667d38523","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/sphod/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"d6bafc9f-93fa-4ed5-bad2-bdfd45ad6274","version":"2.1.284","gitBranch":"main"}
{"parentUuid":"715752f6-a180-4cfc-b1d4-5cc471f278ba","isSidechain":false,"promptId":"947a5e29-19fb-46a7-a6b9-2766d3542841","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01NDCiLZvBV3kpk1BK9ZFUZt","type":"tool_result","content":"File created successfully at: /tmp/claude-1000/-home-sphod-code-iphs400-mp2-cms/d6bafc9f-93fa-4ed5-bad2-bdfd45ad6274/scratchpad/spec.md (file state is current in your context — no need to Read it back)"}]},"uuid":"1df71749-6303-4518-93f9-4a0f4cb56184","timestamp":"2026-10-05T00:48:18.328Z","toolUseResult":{"type":"create","filePath":"/tmp/claude-1000/-home-sphod-code-iphs400-mp2-cms/d6bafc9f-93fa-4ed5-bad2-bdfd45ad6274/scratchpad/spec.md","content":"## Problem Statement\n\nKenyon College Varsity Baseball Team Alumni have no website of their own. Alumni, players, fans, students and parents learn about the team, its schedule and alumni gatherings only by talking to each other. The site is run by the Head Coach (Matt Burdette), who is comfortable using websites but cannot build or edit one, and by a Second Editor (Joe), who looks after the alumni side. Matt must be able to change anything. Joe must never be able to change the Team Schedule. Nobody else may ever edit the site. Alumni who want to be listed must be able to choose it, and their emails must not be exposed to the public internet.\n\n## Solution\n\nA small CMS in two parts, as set by ADR-001 and ADR-002. A private admin console, run on Matt's laptop (the builder's laptop in the demo), lets Matt and Joe log in and manage pages, posts, alumni listings and users. A `cms publish` step renders only the published content into plain, relative-path HTML in `site/`, which is deployed to GitHub Pages.\n\nThe public site has these pages in its navigation: **Home**, **Team Schedule**, **Alumni Events** and **Alumni Network**, plus **About**. Events and Team Updates are posts. Alumni Network shows consented alumni names only, and tells visitors to email Joe to reach an alumnus (ADR-003).\n\n### The 7 required capabilities, in this client's words\n\n1. **Login** - Matt and Joe sign in and out. Passwords are stored as argon2 hashes. A wrong password is refused with a message.\n2. **Two roles** - *admin* (Matt) can do everything. *editor* (Joe) can write and publish any content except **Locked** pages, and cannot manage users.\n3. **Posts** - Events and Team Updates. Create, read, update and delete, with title, slug, Markdown body, draft/published status, Post Type, author and timestamps.\n4. **Pages** - Home, Team Schedule, Alumni Events, Alumni Network, About and any others. Same fields and operations as posts, and a published page appears in the public navigation. The Team Schedule is Locked.\n5. **Admin console** - a dashboard with counts, a content list filterable by status and type, and an editor with a Markdown preview.\n6. **User management** - admin only: create users, change roles, deactivate and reactivate. A deactivated user cannot log in.\n7. **Public site and publish** - `cms publish` writes `site/` with published content only, sanitized Markdown, a navigation built from published pages, and relative paths.\n\n**Listings** are an extension beside these seven, not a replacement. They reuse the CRUD pattern of capability 3 and feed the export in capability 7. They exist because opt-in consent needs its own fields (see ADR-003).\n\n## User Stories\n\n1. As the Head Coach, I want to log in with my email and password, so that only I and Joe can change the site.\n2. As the Head Coach, I want a wrong password to be refused with a clear message, so that I know to try again.\n3. As the Head Coach, I want to log out, so that nobody else at a shared computer can use my session.\n4. As the Head Coach, I want my password stored only as a hash, so that it cannot be read from the database.\n5. As the Second Editor, I want to log in and out like Matt, so that my edits are attributed to me.\n6. As a visitor who is not logged in, I want to be sent to the login page when I try to open an admin address, so that I cannot see or change anything.\n7. As the Head Coach, I want to edit the Team Schedule, so that fans and alumni see the current schedule.\n8. As the Head Coach, I want the Team Schedule to be Locked, so that only I can change it.\n9. As the Second Editor, I want to open the Team Schedule and read it, so that I can see what the public sees.\n10. As the Second Editor, I want to be refused, with a clear message, when I try to edit, publish, unpublish or delete the Team Schedule, so that I know it is not mine to change.\n11. As the Head Coach, I want to lock or unlock any page, so that I can protect other pages if I need to.\n12. As the Second Editor, I want not to see lock or unlock controls, so that I cannot unlock the Team Schedule myself.\n13. As the Head Coach, I want to see counts of posts, pages, drafts and listings on a dashboard, so that I know the state of the site at a glance.\n14. As an editor, I want to filter the content list by status (draft or published) and by type, so that I can find what I need quickly.\n15. As an editor, I want to write a post in Markdown and see a preview before saving, so that I can check how it will look.\n16. As an editor, I want to choose a Post Type of Event or Team Update on every post, so that it appears in the right place.\n17. As the Second Editor, I want new posts to default to Event, so that my usual work needs one fewer click.\n18. As an editor, I want a slug generated from the title that I can change, so that I never have to invent a web address.\n19. As an editor, I want to save a post as a draft, so that I can finish it later without the public seeing it.\n20. As an editor, I want to publish and unpublish a post, so that I control what is public.\n21. As an editor, I want to delete a post, so that mistakes can be removed.\n22. As an editor, I want to see a success or error message after every save, so that I know it worked.\n23. As an editor, I want to see who wrote a post and when it was created and last updated, so that I can tell who changed what.\n24. As the Second Editor, I want to publish my Events and Alumni Network content without the Head Coach's approval, so that alumni news goes out promptly.\n25. As an editor, I want to create, edit, publish, unpublish and delete pages, so that the site's sections stay current.\n26. As a visitor, I want published pages to appear in the site's navigation, so that I can find each section.\n27. As an editor, I want an unpublished page to be missing from the navigation, so that unfinished sections are not linked.\n28. As the Second Editor, I want to add an alumnus as a Listing with name, email, optional class year and a consent date, so that only alumni who asked are on the list.\n29. As the Second Editor, I want each Listing to record who entered it and when the alumnus consented, so that there is a record of the opt-in.\n30. As the Second Editor, I want to remove a Listing or withdraw its consent, so that a name disappears at the next publish.\n31. As the Second Editor, I want a Listing without consent never to be published, so that nobody is listed by mistake.\n32. As an alumnus, I want my email never to appear on the public site, so that I am not exposed to spam.\n33. As a visitor, I want to see an alphabetical list of the alumni who chose to be listed, so that I can see who is in the network.\n34. As a visitor, I want a single line on the Alumni Network page telling me to email Joe to reach an alumnus, so that I know how to get in touch.\n35. As a visitor, I want the Alumni Events page to list Event posts newest first, so that I can see what is coming up.\n36. As a fan, I want the Home page to show a welcome, the latest three Team Updates and recent Events, so that I see news first.\n37. As a visitor, I want links from the Home page to Team Schedule, Alumni Events and Alumni Network, so that I can reach each section quickly.\n38. As a visitor on a phone, I want every public page to fit a narrow screen without sideways scrolling, so that I can read it anywhere.\n39. As the Head Coach, I want to create a user and give them the editor or admin role, so that I can add a helper.\n40. As the Head Coach, I want to change a user's role, so that I can adjust what they may do.\n41. As the Head Coach, I want to deactivate a user, so that a former helper can no longer log in.\n42. As the Head Coach, I want to reactivate a user, so that returning helpers get their access back.\n43. As the Head Coach, I want users never to be deleted, so that authors and \"recorded by\" always point to a real person.\n44. As the Head Coach, I want to be unable to deactivate or demote myself, so that the site can never be left with no admin.\n45. As the Second Editor, I want to be refused the Users screen, so that only the Head Coach manages accounts.\n46. As a deactivated user, I want to be refused at login with a message, so that I understand why.\n47. As the Head Coach, I want every form to carry a CSRF token, so that another website cannot change content through my session.\n48. As an editor, I want any `<script>` or `onerror=` in my Markdown stripped, in the preview and on the public site, so that nobody is attacked through the content.\n49. As the Head Coach, I want `cms publish` to write only published content, so that drafts never leave the database.\n50. As the Head Coach, I want published pages to use relative paths, so that the site works from the GitHub Pages subfolder.\n51. As the Head Coach, I want edits to stay private until I run publish, so that I choose when the public sees a change.\n52. As a grader, I want a seed script that fills the database with demo data from `.env.example` values, so that the app can be walked through from a clean clone.\n\n## Implementation Decisions\n\n- **Two seams.** Behaviour is exposed through the HTTP application and through the publish export. Nothing else is a public interface.\n- **Content model.** Three content types: Page, Post and Listing. Plus Users.\n  - Pages and Posts share title, slug, Markdown body, status (draft or published), author and timestamps.\n  - Posts add a required Post Type: Event or Team Update.\n  - Pages add a Locked flag, changeable only by an admin.\n  - Listings carry name, email, optional class year, consent date, and recorded-by.\n- **Roles.** Two roles: admin and editor. A single permission check guards every route.\n  - Editors cannot reach user management.\n  - Editors can open a Locked page but cannot edit, publish, unpublish or delete it.\n  - Anonymous visitors are redirected to login.\n- **Users.** Users are deactivated, never deleted. A deactivated user cannot log in. An admin cannot deactivate or demote themselves.\n- **Login and sessions.** Passwords hashed with argon2. Signed session cookies. The login form takes email and password. Secrets are read from the environment.\n- **CSRF.** Every state-changing form carries a token, and a POST without a valid token is rejected.\n- **Markdown.** Rendered then sanitized before it is shown anywhere, including the admin preview, per the CLAUDE.md hard constraints.\n- **Admin console.** A dashboard of counts, a content list filterable by status and type, an editor with a preview, a Listings screen and a Users screen. Persistent navigation and a success or error message after each save.\n- **Special pages.** The exporter gives the Home, Alumni Events and Alumni Network pages their generated sections: recent posts, the Event list, and the consented names. The page's own Markdown is rendered above them.\n- **Alumni data.** The exporter never reads the Listing email field (ADR-003). Only consented Listings are exported, names only, in alphabetical order.\n- **Publish.** `cms publish` renders published Pages, Posts and consented Listings into `site/` with relative paths only. `site/` is generated and never edited by hand. Drafts, unconsented Listings and emails are never exported.\n- **Deployment.** `cms deploy` pushes `site/` to the `gh-pages` branch. The admin console is never deployed (ADR-001).\n- **Demo data.** The seed script creates Matt as admin, Joe as editor, the five pages with Team Schedule Locked, sample posts of both types, and sample Listings.\n\n## Testing Decisions\n\n- **What makes a good test.** It asserts external behaviour only: an HTTP status, a redirect, a message, a file's content. It does not reach into internals.\n- **HTTP seam (most tests).** Use the existing `client` and `client_as(role)` fixtures and the app factory. Cover login and logout, roles, Locked pages, Post, Page and Listing CRUD, user management and deactivation, the content list filters, and the preview.\n  - **Persistence.** Post and Page CRUD must survive a restart: build a fresh app on the same database file and check the content is still there.\n  - **Access control.** Enumerate the admin-only routes from the app's own router, and assert an editor gets 403 or a redirect and an anonymous visitor is redirected to login, so a route added later cannot escape the check.\n  - **CSRF.** Replay each state-changing POST without a token and assert rejection, one per form.\n  - **Password hashing.** Read the stored hash and assert it is an argon2 hash, never plain text.\n- **Publish seam.** Run the export into a temporary directory and assert on the files:\n  - Drafts and unconsented Listings are absent.\n  - No alumnus email appears anywhere in the output.\n  - Every path is relative, with no `href=\"/` or `src=\"/`, and every internal link resolves.\n  - A `<script>` tag and an `onerror=` attribute are stripped.\n  - The navigation is built from published pages only, and Home, Alumni Events and the Alumni Network list are generated correctly.\n- **Seed script.** A test runs it from `.env.example` values and checks it completes with no manual steps.\n- **Prior art.** The existing walking-skeleton test and the `client_as` helper, whose docstring already shows the access-control pattern.\n- **Traceability.** Each ticket's acceptance criteria should be named by at least one test.\n\n## Out of Scope\n\n- Alumni accounts or logins, because the public site is static.\n- Comments.\n- Media uploads.\n- A public sign-up form for alumni.\n- A hosted admin console on the internet.\n- RSVPs, because a static site cannot take submissions.\n- Scheduled or recurring events.\n- A structured game and results database. {"parentUuid":"41f04c89-7e20-406d-bd69-ddbf6a9c2b6d","isSidechain":false,"message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-5-5","id":"msg_011CfiAq8i1smpftxoXAnPat","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The spec is published as issue #1: https://github.com/samuelphodges/iphs400-mp2-cms/issues/1. It also covers the full template, with 52 user stories, implementation and testing decisions, and the seams we agreed on, including the four additions.\n\n**Please read the Further Notes section on GitHub before `/to-tickets`.** I filled in a few things we never discussed, and they'll pass into every ticket unless you correct them:\n- Slugs are generated from the title and editable.\n- Navigation shows Home first, then the other published pages alphabetically.\n- Home shows three Team Updates and three recent Events.\n- Home, Alumni Events and Alumni Network are recognised by fixed slugs.\n- Joe's public contact address is a site setting, not part of a Listing.\n\nTell me which to change and I'll edit the issue. {"parentUuid":"5bb05179-04ae-428f-993f-e9520dc393ff","isSidechain":false,"promptId":"b5142c74-18fe-4fb1-baf3-2400f5d09064","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":"<command-message>mattpocock-skills:to-tickets</command-message>\n<command-name>/mattpocock-skills:to-tickets</command-name>"},"uuid":"99e4feee-d1b3-4e5c-ac6f-e839b76abc31","timestamp":"2026-10-05T00:52:57.401Z","origin":{"kind":"human"},"turnOrigin":"human","turnPosition":{"promptIndex":15,"turnIndex":15},"userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/sphod/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"d6bafc9f-93fa-4ed5-bad2-bdfd45ad6274","version":"2.1.284","gitBranch":"main"}
{"parentUuid":"99e4feee-d1b3-4e5c-ac6f-e839b76abc31","isSidechain":false,"promptId":"b5142c74-18fe-4fb1-baf3-2400f5d09064","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Base directory for this skill: /home/sphod/.claude/plugins/cache/claude-plugins-official/mattpocock-skills/1.2.3/skills/engineering/to-tickets\n\n# To Tickets\n\nBreak a plan, spec, or conversation into a set of **tickets**: tracer-bullet vertical slices, each declaring the tickets that **block** it.\n\nThe issue tracker and triage label vocabulary should have been provided to you. If not, tell the user to run `/setup-matt-pocock-skills`.\n\n## Process\n\n### 1. Gather context\n\nWork from whatever is already in the conversation context. If the user passes a reference (a spec path, an issue number or URL) as an argument, fetch it and read its full body and comments.\n\n### 2. Explore the codebase (optional)\n\nIf you have not already explored the codebase, do so to understand the current state of the code. Ticket titles and descriptions should use the project's domain glossary vocabulary, and respect ADRs in the area you're touching.\n\nLook for opportunities to prefactor the code to make the implementation easier. \"Make the change easy, then make the easy change.\"\n\n### 3. Draft vertical slices\n\nBreak the work into **tracer bullet** tickets.\n\n<vertical-slice-rules>\n\n- Each slice cuts a narrow but COMPLETE path through every layer (schema, API, UI, tests): vertical, NOT a horizontal slice of one layer\n- A completed slice is demoable or verifiable on its own\n- Each slice is sized to fit in a single fresh context window\n- Any prefactoring should be done first\n\n</vertical-slice-rules>\n\nGive each ticket its **blocking edges**: the other tickets that must complete before it can start. A ticket with no blockers can start immediately.\n\n**Wide refactors are the exception to vertical slicing.** A **wide refactor** is one mechanical change (rename a column, retype a shared symbol) whose **blast radius** fans across the whole codebase, so a single edit breaks thousands of call sites at once and no vertical slice can land green. Don't force it into a tracer bullet; sequence it as **expand–contract**. First expand: add the new form beside the old so nothing breaks. Then migrate the call sites over in batches sized by blast radius (per package, per directory), each batch its own ticket blocked by the expand, keeping CI green batch to batch because the old form still exists. Finally contract: delete the old form once no caller remains, in a ticket blocked by every migrate batch. When even the batches can't stay green alone, keep the sequence but let them share an integration branch that all block a final integrate-and-verify ticket; green is promised only there.\n\n### 4. Quiz the user\n\nPresent the proposed breakdown as a numbered list. For each ticket, show:\n\n- **Title**: short descriptive name\n- **Blocked by**: which other tickets (if any) must complete first\n- **What it delivers**: the end-to-end behaviour this ticket makes work\n\nAsk the user:\n\n- Does the granularity feel right? (too coarse / too fine)\n- Are the blocking edges correct: does each ticket only depend on tickets that genuinely gate it?\n- Should any tickets be merged or split further?\n\nIterate until the user approves the breakdown.\n\n### 5. Publish the tickets to the configured tracker\n\nPublish the approved tickets. **How** depends on the tracker `/setup-matt-pocock-skills` configured; the tickets are the same either way, only the shape of the blocking edges changes:\n\n- **Local files** → write one file per ticket under `.scratch/<feature-slug>/issues/<NN>-<slug>.md`, numbered from `01` in dependency order (blockers first). Each file's \"Blocked by\" lists the numbers/titles it depends on. Use the per-ticket file template below: one ticket per file, never a single combined file.\n- **A real issue tracker (GitHub, Linear, …)** → publish one issue per ticket in dependency order (blockers first) so each ticket's blocking edges can reference real identifiers. Use the platform's native blocking / sub-issue relationship where it has one; otherwise set each ticket's \"Blocked by\" to the blocking issues. Apply the `ready-for-agent` triage label unless instructed otherwise; the tickets are agent-grabbable by construction.\n\nWork the **frontier**: any ticket whose blockers are all done. For a purely linear chain that means top to bottom.\n\nDo NOT close or modify any parent issue.\n\n<local-ticket-template>\n\n# <NN>: <Ticket title>\n\n**What to build:** the end-to-end behaviour this ticket makes work, from the user's perspective, not a layer-by-layer implementation list.\n\n**Blocked by:** the numbers/titles of the tickets that gate this one, or \"None (can start immediately)\".\n\n**Status:** ready-for-agent\n\n- [ ] Acceptance criterion 1\n- [ ] Acceptance criterion 2\n\n</local-ticket-template>\n\n<issue-template>\n\n## Parent\n\nA reference to the parent issue on the tracker (if the source was an existing issue, otherwise omit this section).\n\n## What to build\n\nThe end-to-end behaviour this ticket makes work, from the user's perspective, not layer-by-layer implementation.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] Criterion 1\n- [ ] Criterion 2\n\n## Blocked by\n\n- A reference to each blocking ticket, or \"None (can start immediately)\".\n\n</issue-template>\n\nIn either form, avoid specific file paths or code snippets: they go stale fast. Exception: if a prototype produced a snippet that encodes a decision more precisely than prose can (state machine, reducer, schema, type shape), inline it and note briefly that it came from a prototype. Trim to the decision-rich parts, not a working demo, just the important bits.\n"}]},"isMeta":true,"turnCompanion":true,"uuid":"a2a71f55-752d-4020-9540-e75ee580bf7e","timestamp":"2026-10-05T00:52:57.401Z","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/sphod/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"d6bafc9f-93fa-4ed5-bad2-bdfd45ad6274","version":"2.1.284","gitBranch":"main"}

{"parentUuid":"bc69c00d-fec7-4c3c-b553-b7658aac1d38","isSidechain":false,"message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-5-5","id":"msg_011CfiBA5anD4rTeWX8Fzmee","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Here is the proposed breakdown: 8 tickets, inside the manual's 7–9 range. T00, the walking skeleton, is already done. Each ticket is a vertical slice that can be demoed alone, and I've put the Stage 1 deadline in mind: T01–T03 end with a published item live on GitHub Pages.\n\n**T01 – Matt and Joe can sign in and out**\n- **Blocked by:** None.\n- **Delivers:** seeded Matt (admin) and Joe (editor), argon2-hashed passwords, login and logout, a clear message on a wrong password, and anonymous visitors sent to login from any admin address. The login form carries a CSRF token. This is capabilities 1 and part of 2.\n\n**T02 – Editors can write, preview and manage pages**\n- **Blocked by:** T01.\n- **Delivers:** Page create, edit, publish, unpublish and delete with title, slug (generated, editable), Markdown body, status, author and timestamps. It also has the editor preview with sanitized output, success and error messages, and a first content list of pages. This is capability 4 in the admin, and the start of capability 5.\n\n**T03 – Publish pages to the public site and put it on GitHub Pages**\n- **Blocked by:** T02.\n- **Delivers:** `cms publish` writes only published pages to `site/`, with relative paths and sanitized Markdown, and Home first in a navigation built from the other published pages alphabetically. Deploying it makes the site live on Pages. This is capability 7's core, and it's what the Stage 1 \"live on Pages\" item needs.\n\n**T04 – Editors can write Events and Team Updates**\n- **Blocked by:** T02, T03.\n- **Delivers:** Post CRUD with the required Post Type. Events default for Joe. The content list filters by status and type. Publish adds the Event list to Alumni Events (newest first), and Home shows the latest three Team Updates and three recent Events. This is capability 3 and more of 5 and 7.\n\n**T05 – Only Matt can change Locked pages**\n- **Blocked by:** T02.\n- **Delivers:** the Locked flag, set on the Team Schedule when seeded. Joe can open it but is refused if he tries to edit, publish, unpublish or delete it, with a clear refusal page. Only Matt sees lock and unlock. It includes the access-control tests that enumerate admin-only routes from the router. This is capability 2 in full.\n\n**T06 – Matt can manage users**\n- **Blocked by:** T01.\n- **Delivers:** a Users screen, admin only, to create users, change roles, deactivate and reactivate. A deactivated user can't log in. Matt can't deactivate or demote himself, and users are never deleted. Joe is refused the screen. This is capability 6.\n\n**T07 – Joe can keep the Alumni Network, with names only**\n- **Blocked by:** T03.\n- **Delivers:** Listing CRUD (name, email, optional class year, consent date, recorded-by), with an alumni screen in the admin. Publish adds the alphabetical list of consented names and the Alumni Contact Line to Alumni Network. A test asserts no alumnus email appears anywhere in `site/`. This is ADR-003.\n\n**T08 – Dashboard, hardening and demo data**\n- **Blocked by:** T04, T05, T06, T07.\n- **Delivers:** the dashboard with counts of posts, pages, drafts and listings, and persistent admin navigation. It also adds a CSRF-replay test for every form, a link and relative-path check on the whole export, a demo seed that runs clean from `.env.example`, and a check at 390 px width. This closes capability 5 and the cross-cutting checks the rubric asks for.\n\n**A few things for you to check:**\n- **Granularity:** T02, T04 and T07 are the biggest. If any looks too big for one session, I'd split T04 into \"Posts CRUD and list filters\" and \"Posts on Home and Alumni Events\".\n- **Merging:** T05 and T06 are small and independent. I kept them separate so each can be demoed on its own, but they could merge into one \"roles and users\" ticket.\n- **Blocking edges:** T05 depends only on T02, so it can run before T03 lands. T06 depends only on T01.\n- **T08 is the cross-cutting one.** If you'd rather not have a catch-all, the CSRF replay could be a test in each ticket instead.\n\nDoes the granularity feel right, and are the blocking edges correct? Once you approve, I'll publish them in dependency order, so each ticket can reference real issue numbers in its \"Blocked by\" line."}],"container":null,"stop_reason":"end_turn","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":4,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1957,"cache_read_input_tokens":99262,"output_tokens":2620,"output_tokens_details":{"thinking_tokens":1079},"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":1957,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0},"inference_geo":"not_available","iterations":[{"input_tokens":4,"output_tokens":2620,"cache_read_input_tokens":99262,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1957,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":1957},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"serverClassifierRequest":"0773a209-3264-476d-b18a-36852c1cfcd8","apiBlockIndex":1,"requestId":"req_011CfiBA5BDEC1kyT2hYhqKD","attributionSkill":"mattpocock-skills:to-tickets","attributionPlugin":"mattpocock-skills","type":"assistant","uuid":"f214aea5-4d32-48da-ab73-e07cae13c7ad","timestamp":"2026-10-05T00:53:18.605Z","effort":"medium","perTurnEffort":"medium","session_id":"9d3ee875-b856-4c5f-8263-d5e667d38523","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/sphod/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"d6bafc9f-93fa-4ed5-bad2-bdfd45ad6274","version":"2.1.284","gitBranch":"main"}

{"parentUuid":"fdd30c81-98e2-429f-95ff-4c48fe85593b","isSidechain":false,"message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-5-5","id":"msg_011CfiBPwFrr8TASqzARCEUH","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_016eVCoyTTiBUwmzzNeMiE9p","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/home/sphod/code/iphs400-mp2-cms/docs/handoff/01_tickets-approved-not-published_20261004.md","content":"# Handoff: tickets approved, not yet published\n\n**Date:** 2026-10-04 · **Phase:** `tickets` (see `.claude/state/phase`)\n\n## State\n\n- Grill is done. `CONTEXT.md` has the glossary (15 terms), and ADR-003 is written.\n- Spec is published as issue #1 (labels `spec`, `ready-for-agent`). The user confirmed the\n  five defaults listed in its Further Notes.\n- Labels `spec`, `ticket`, `stretch`, `ready-for-agent`, `needs-triage`, `needs-info` and\n  `ready-for-human` exist on the repo.\n- The user approved the 8-ticket breakdown below. **None are published yet.**\n\n## Next step\n\nPublish the tickets with `/to-tickets` (or by hand with `gh issue create`), in dependency order, labelled `ticket` and\n`ready-for-agent`, each with a \"What to build\", \"Acceptance criteria\" and a \"Blocked by\"\nsection using real issue numbers. Use the title form `T0N: ...`. Do not close or edit issue #1.\n\n## Approved breakdown\n\n| Ticket | Title | Blocked by |\n|---|---|---|\n| T01 | Matt and Joe can sign in and out | None |\n| T02 | Editors can write, preview and manage pages | T01 |\n| T03 | Publish pages to the public site and put it on GitHub Pages | T02 |\n| T04 | Editors can write Events and Team Updates | T02, T03 |\n| T05 | Only Matt can change Locked pages | T02 |\n| T06 | Matt can manage users | T01 |\n| T07 | Joe can keep the Alumni Network, with names only | T03 |\n| T08 | Dashboard, hardening and demo data | T04, T05, T06, T07 |\n\n- **T01:** seeded Matt (admin) and Joe (editor); argon2; login/logout; wrong-password message; anonymous redirect; CSRF on the login form.\n- **T02:** Page CRUD; generated, editable slug; sanitized Markdown preview; success/error messages; first content list of pages.\n- **T03:** `cms publish` writes only published pages with relative paths; Home first, the other pages alphabetical in the nav; deploy to Pages. Needed for Stage 1 \"live on Pages\".\n- **T04:** Post CRUD with required Post Type (Event default for Joe); list filters by status and type; Alumni Events lists Events newest first; Home shows 3 Team Updates and 3 recent Events.\n- **T05:** Locked flag, set on the Team Schedule; Joe can open but is refused edit, publish, unpublish and delete; only Matt sees lock/unlock; access-control tests that enumerate admin-only routes from the router.\n- **T06:** Users screen (admin only): create, change role, deactivate/reactivate; deactivated users cannot log in; Matt cannot deactivate or demote himself; users are never deleted.\n- **T07:** Listing CRUD (name, email, optional class year, consent date, recorded-by); publish adds the alphabetical consented names and the Alumni Contact Line; a test asserts no email appears in `site/`.\n- **T08:** dashboard counts; persistent admin nav; CSRF replay test per form; link and relative-path check on the whole export; seed script runs clean from `.env.example`; 390 px width check.\n\n## Open questions the user raised\n\n- Granularity: T04 could split into \"Posts CRUD and list filters\" and \"Posts on Home and Alumni Events\". User said granularity felt right; keep as is unless a ticket overruns its context.\n\n## Reminders\n\n- Save this session's transcript to `docs/transcripts/` (naming pattern in the rubric).\n- Stage 1 needs T01-T03 closed (each with a `/code-review` comment before close) and a live Pages item.\n- One ticket per fresh session; `/clear` between tickets.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"container":null,"stop_reason":"tool_use","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":2697,"cache_read_input_tokens":101219,"output_tokens":1774,"output_tokens_details":{"thinking_tokens":307},"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":2697,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0},"inference_geo":"not_available","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":1774,"cache_read_input_tokens":101219,"cache_creation_input_tokens":2697,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":2697},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"wireToolInputs":{"toolu_016eVCoyTTiBUwmzzNeMiE9p":{"file_path":"/home/sphod/code/iphs400-mp2-cms/docs/handoff/01_tickets-approved-not-published_20261004.md","content":"# Handoff: tickets approved, not yet published\n\n**Date:** 2026-10-04 · **Phase:** `tickets` (see `.claude/state/phase`)\n\n## State\n\n- Grill is done. `CONTEXT.md` has the glossary (15 terms), and ADR-003 is written.\n- Spec is published as issue #1 (labels `spec`, `ready-for-agent`). {"parentUuid":"1f9d9832-f555-48b3-8d56-031fe722f3f9","isSidechain":false,"message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-5-5","id":"msg_011CfjvineDuPMvRQDHKpTUz","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01PFEgii6co69dkC9AV1oRhn","name":"Bash","input":{"command":"S=/tmp/claude-1000/-home-sphod-code-iphs400-mp2-cms/d6bafc9f-93fa-4ed5-bad2-bdfd45ad6274/scratchpad\ncat > $S/t02.md <<'EOF'\n## Parent\n\nSpec: #1\n\n## What to build\n\nEditors can write and manage pages in the admin console. A page has a title, a generated and editable slug, a Markdown body, a draft or published status, an author and timestamps. The editor shows a preview of the sanitized Markdown, and every save shows a success or error message. A first content list shows the pages. This is capability 4 in the admin and the start of capability 5.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] A logged-in editor or admin can create a page with title, Markdown body and status; the slug is generated from the title and can be changed.\n- [ ] Two pages cannot share a slug; the second save shows an error.\n- [ ] A page can be edited, published, unpublished and deleted; each action shows a success message.\n- [ ] Author, created and updated timestamps are recorded and shown.\n- [ ] Pages persist across a server restart.\n- [ ] The editor shows a preview of the Markdown, and a `<script>` tag or `onerror=` attribute in the body does not appear in the preview.\n- [ ] A content list shows all pages with title, status and last update.\n- [ ] Every page form carries a CSRF token and rejects a POST without one.\n- [ ] An anonymous visitor is redirected to login on every page route.\n\n## Blocked by\n\n- #2 (T01)\nEOF\ncat > $S/t03.md <<'EOF'\n## Parent\n\nSpec: #1\n\n## What to build\n\n`cms publish` writes the published pages to `site/` as plain HTML, and `cms deploy` puts the result on GitHub Pages so the site is live. Only published pages are exported, Markdown is sanitized, and the navigation is built from the published pages (Home first, the others alphabetical). All paths are relative. This is the core of capability 7 and what the Stage 1 \"live on Pages\" item needs.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] `cms publish` writes one HTML page per published page into `site/`; drafts are not written.\n- [ ] Unpublishing a page and re-running publish removes it from `site/`.\n- [ ] Every published page includes a navigation listing the published pages, Home first and the rest alphabetical.\n- [ ] Markdown is sanitized: a `<script>` tag and an `onerror=` attribute in a body are absent from the exported HTML.\n- [ ] No exported file contains `href=\"/` or `src=\"/`, and every internal link resolves to a file in `site/`.\n- [ ] The public CSS and links work when the site is served from a subfolder.\n- [ ] `cms deploy` pushes `site/` to the `gh-pages` branch, and at least one published page is live at the Pages URL over HTTPS.\n- [ ] `site/` is generated output and is not edited by hand.\n\n## Blocked by\n\n- #3 (T02)\nEOF\nn2=$($S/mk.sh \"T02: Editors can write, preview and manage pages\" $S/t02.md) || { echo FAIL T02; exit 1; }\nn3=$($S/mk.sh \"T03: Publish pages to the public site and put it on GitHub Pages\" $S/t03.md) || { echo FAIL T03; exit 1; }\necho T02=#$n2 T03=#$n3; echo \"$n2 $n3\" > $S/n23","description":"Publish T02 and T03"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"container":null,"stop_reason":"tool_use","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1210,"cache_read_input_tokens":107173,"output_tokens":1265,"output_tokens_details":{"thinking_tokens":0},"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":1210,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0},"inference_geo":"not_available","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":1265,"cache_read_input_tokens":107173,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1210,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":1210},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"wireToolInputs":{"toolu_01PFEgii6co69dkC9AV1oRhn":{"command":"cd /home/sphod/code/iphs400-mp2-cms && S=/tmp/claude-1000/-home-sphod-code-iphs400-mp2-cms/d6bafc9f-93fa-4ed5-bad2-bdfd45ad6274/scratchpad\ncat > $S/t02.md <<'EOF'\n## Parent\n\nSpec: #1\n\n## What to build\n\nEditors can write and manage pages in the admin console. A page has a title, a generated and editable slug, a Markdown body, a draft or published status, an author and timestamps. The editor shows a preview of the sanitized Markdown, and every save shows a success or error message. A first content list shows the pages. This is capability 4 in the admin and the start of capability 5.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] A logged-in editor or admin can create a page with title, Markdown body and status; the slug is generated from the title and can be changed.\n- [ ] Two pages cannot share a slug; the second save shows an error.\n- [ ] A page can be edited, published, unpublished and deleted; each action shows a success message.\n- [ ] Author, created and updated timestamps are recorded and shown.\n- [ ] Pages persist across a server restart.\n- [ ] The editor shows a preview of the Markdown, and a `<script>` tag or `onerror=` attribute in the body does not appear in the preview.\n- [ ] A content list shows all pages with title, status and last update.\n- [ ] Every page form carries a CSRF token and rejects a POST without one.\n- [ ] An anonymous visitor is redirected to login on every page route.\n\n## Blocked by\n\n- #2 (T01)\nEOF\ncat > $S/t03.md <<'EOF'\n## Parent\n\nSpec: #1\n\n## What to build\n\n`cms publish` writes the published pages to `site/` as plain HTML, and `cms deploy` puts the result on GitHub Pages so the site is live. Only published pages are exported, Markdown is sanitized, and the navigation is built from the published pages (Home first, the others alphabetical). All paths are relative. This is the core of capability 7 and what the Stage 1 \"live on Pages\" item needs.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] `cms publish` writes one HTML page per published page into `site/`; drafts are not written.\n- [ ] Unpublishing a page and re-running publish removes it from `site/`.\n- [ ] Every published page includes a navigation listing the published pages, Home first and the rest alphabetical.\n- [ ] Markdown is sanitized: a `<script>` tag and an `onerror=` attribute in a body are absent from the exported HTML.\n- [ ] No exported file contains `href=\"/` or `src=\"/`, and every internal link resolves to a file in `site/`.\n- [ ] The public CSS and links work when the site is served from a subfolder.\n- [ ] `cms deploy` pushes `site/` to the `gh-pages` branch, and at least one published page is live at the Pages URL over HTTPS.\n- [ ] `site/` is generated output and is not edited by hand.\n\n## Blocked by\n\n- #3 (T02)\nEOF\nn2=$($S/mk.sh \"T02: Editors can write, preview and manage pages\" $S/t02.md) || { echo FAIL T02; exit 1; }\nn3=$($S/mk.sh \"T03: Publish pages to the public site and put it on GitHub Pages\" $S/t03.md) || { echo FAIL T03; exit 1; }\necho T02=#$n2 T03=#$n3; echo \"$n2 $n3\" > $S/n23","description":"Publish T02 and T03"}},"wireIngestContext":{"toolu_01PFEgii6co69dkC9AV1oRhn":{"cwd":"/home/sphod/code/iphs400-mp2-cms"}},"serverClassifierRequest":"b6e68730-0ed7-4141-94cb-ea7c8da99de6","apiBlockIndex":0,"requestId":"req_011CfjvinPrPzECT5tYDAnkZ","type":"assistant","uuid":"abe44051-1d16-423d-9e40-4a9375cc26c3","timestamp":"2026-10-05T23:04:59.083Z","effort":"medium","perTurnEffort":"medium","session_id":"9d3ee875-b856-4c5f-8263-d5e667d38523","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/sphod/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"d6bafc9f-93fa-4ed5-bad2-bdfd45ad6274","version":"2.1.284","gitBranch":"main"}

{"parentUuid":"5f8c053d-b275-45c2-bbaa-31349adccf60","isSidechain":false,"message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-5-5","id":"msg_011CfjvjiagnUyvJDbfV5mKS","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01UYF7NsgmvN1638scpyrSkU","name":"Bash","input":{"command":"S=/tmp/claude-1000/-home-sphod-code-iphs400-mp2-cms/d6bafc9f-93fa-4ed5-bad2-bdfd45ad6274/scratchpad\ncat > $S/t04.md <<'EOF'\n## Parent\n\nSpec: #1\n\n## What to build\n\nEditors can write Events and Team Updates as posts. Every post has a required Post Type, and Events are the default for Joe. The content list filters by status and type. At publish, Alumni Events lists the Event posts newest first, and Home shows the latest three Team Updates and three recent Events. This is capability 3 and more of capabilities 5 and 7.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] An editor can create, edit, publish, unpublish and delete a post with title, generated and editable slug, Markdown body, status, Post Type, author and timestamps.\n- [ ] Post Type is required and is Event or Team Update; a new post by an editor defaults to Event.\n- [ ] Posts persist across a server restart.\n- [ ] The post editor shows a sanitized Markdown preview.\n- [ ] The content list filters by status and by type, and shows posts and pages together.\n- [ ] After publish, the Alumni Events page lists published Events newest first and omits drafts and Team Updates.\n- [ ] After publish, Home shows the latest three published Team Updates and three recent published Events, with links to Team Schedule, Alumni Events and Alumni Network.\n- [ ] Every post form carries a CSRF token and rejects a POST without one.\n- [ ] Exported post content is sanitized and uses relative paths.\n\n## Blocked by\n\n- #3 (T02)\n- #4 (T03)\nEOF\ncat > $S/t05.md <<'EOF'\n## Parent\n\nSpec: #1\n\n## What to build\n\nOnly Matt can change Locked pages. A page has a Locked flag, and the Team Schedule is seeded Locked. Joe can open a Locked page to read it but is refused, with a clear message, if he tries to edit, publish, unpublish or delete it. Only Matt sees lock and unlock. This also adds the access-control tests that cover every admin-only route. This completes capability 2.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] A page has a Locked flag; the seeded Team Schedule page is Locked.\n- [ ] An admin can edit, publish, unpublish and delete a Locked page, and can lock or unlock any page.\n- [ ] An editor can open a Locked page and see its content, but save, publish, unpublish and delete are all refused with a clear refusal page.\n- [ ] An editor sees no lock or unlock control, and a forged request to change the flag is refused.\n- [ ] An editor can still edit and publish every unlocked page and post.\n- [ ] Tests build the admin-only route list from the app's router and assert an editor gets 403 or a redirect, and an anonymous visitor is redirected to login, on every one.\n- [ ] The refusal page is reachable for screenshots.\n\n## Blocked by\n\n- #3 (T02)\nEOF\ncat > $S/t06.md <<'EOF'\n## Parent\n\nSpec: #1\n\n## What to build\n\nMatt can manage users on an admin-only Users screen: create a user, change a role, deactivate and reactivate. A deactivated user cannot log in. Users are never deleted, and Matt cannot deactivate or demote himself, so the site is never left without an admin. Joe is refused the screen. This is capability 6.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] An admin can create a user with name, email, password and role (admin or editor); the password is stored as an argon2 hash.\n- [ ] An admin can change a user's role.\n- [ ] An admin can deactivate and reactivate a user.\n- [ ] A deactivated user is refused at login with a clear message, and an already-open session is no longer accepted.\n- [ ] There is no way to delete a user.\n- [ ] An admin cannot deactivate or demote their own account; the attempt shows an error.\n- [ ] An editor gets 403 or a redirect on every Users route, and an anonymous visitor is redirected to login.\n- [ ] Every Users form carries a CSRF token and rejects a POST without one.\n\n## Blocked by\n\n- #2 (T01)\nEOF\ncat > $S/t07.md <<'EOF'\n## Parent\n\nSpec: #1\n\n## What to build\n\nJoe can keep the Alumni Network. A Listing is one alumnus who has chosen to be listed, with name, email, optional class year, consent date and who recorded it. At publish, the Alumni Network page shows the alphabetical list of consented names plus the Alumni Contact Line telling visitors to email Joe. Emails are never published (ADR-003).\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] An editor can create, edit and delete a Listing with name, email, optional class year and consent date; recorded-by is set to the logged-in user.\n- [ ] Listings persist across a server restart.\n- [ ] A Listing without a consent date cannot be saved as consented, and is not published.\n- [ ] After publish, the Alumni Network page shows the names of consented Listings in alphabetical order, below the page's own Markdown text.\n- [ ] Deleting a Listing or removing its consent, then publishing again, removes the name from `site/`.\n- [ ] The Alumni Contact Line appears on the Alumni Network page and uses Joe's address from a site setting.\n- [ ] A test asserts that no alumnus email appears anywhere in `site/`.\n- [ ] Every Listing form carries a CSRF token and rejects a POST without one; anonymous visitors are redirected to login.\n\n## Blocked by\n\n- #4 (T03)\nEOF\ncat > $S/t08.md <<'EOF'\n## Parent\n\nSpec: #1\n\n## What to build\n\nThe finishing slice for the admin and the whole site. The dashboard shows counts, the admin navigation is persistent, and demo data lets a grader walk through everything from a clean clone. It also adds the checks that cut across all forms and pages. This closes capability 5 and the cross-cutting rubric items.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] The dashboard shows counts of posts, pages, drafts and listings, and the counts change when content changes.\n- [ ] A persistent admin navigation links to Dashboard, Content, Listings and Users; Users is hidden from editors.\n- [ ] A test replays every state-changing POST in the app without a CSRF token and asserts rejection.\n- [ ] A test crawls the exported `site/` and asserts that every internal link resolves and no `href=\"/` or `src=\"/` appears.\n- [ ] The seed script runs clean from `.env.example` values with no manual steps and creates Matt, Joe, the five pages with Team Schedule Locked, sample Events and Team Updates, and sample Listings.\n- [ ] The public Home page and three admin screens have no horizontal overflow at 390 px.\n- [ ] The full test suite passes on a clean clone.\n\n## Blocked by\n\n- #5 (T04)\n- #6 (T05)\n- #7 (T06)\n- #8 (T07)\nEOF\nn4=$($S/mk.sh \"T04: Editors can write Events and Team Updates\" $S/t04.md) || { echo FAIL T04; Start implementing at T01 (#2).\n\n## Next step (done)","originalFile":"# Handoff: tickets approved, not yet published\n\n**Date:** 2026-10-04 · **Phase:** `tickets` (see `.claude/state/phase`)\n\n## State\n\n- Grill is done. `CONTEXT.md` has the glossary (15 terms), and ADR-003 is written.\n- Spec is published as issue #1 (labels `spec`, `ready-for-agent`). The user confirmed the\n  five defaults listed in its Further Notes.\n- Labels `spec`, `ticket`, `stretch`, `ready-for-agent`, `needs-triage`, `needs-info` and\n  `ready-for-human` exist on the repo.\n- The user approved the 8-ticket breakdown below. **None are published yet.**\n\n## Next step\n\nPublish the tickets with `/to-tickets` (or by hand with `gh issue create`), in dependency order, labelled `ticket` and\n`ready-for-agent`, each with a \"What to build\", \"Acceptance criteria\" and a \"Blocked by\"\nsection using real issue numbers. Use the title form `T0N: ...`. Do not close or edit issue #1.\n\n## Approved breakdown\n\n| Ticket | Title | Blocked by |\n|---|---|---|\n| T01 | Matt and Joe can sign in and out | None |\n| T02 | Editors can write, preview and manage pages | T01 |\n| T03 | Publish pages to the public site and put it on GitHub Pages | T02 |\n| T04 | Editors can write Events and Team Updates | T02, T03 |\n| T05 | Only Matt can change Locked pages | T02 |\n| T06 | Matt can manage users | T01 |\n| T07 | Joe can keep the Alumni Network, with names only | T03 |\n| T08 | Dashboard, hardening and demo data | T04, T05, T06, T07 |\n\n- **T01:** seeded Matt (admin) and Joe (editor); argon2; login/logout; wrong-password message; anonymous redirect; CSRF on the login form.\n- **T02:** Page CRUD; generated, editable slug; sanitized Markdown preview; success/error messages; first content list of pages.\n- **T03:** `cms publish` writes only published pages with relative paths; Home first, the other pages alphabetical in the nav; deploy to Pages. Needed for Stage 1 \"live on Pages\".\n- **T04:** Post CRUD with required Post Type (Event default for Joe); list filters by status and type; Alumni Events lists Events newest first; Home shows 3 Team Updates and 3 recent Events.\n- **T05:** Locked flag, set on the Team Schedule; Joe can open but is refused edit, publish, unpublish and delete; only Matt sees lock/unlock; access-control tests that enumerate admin-only routes from the router.\n- **T06:** Users screen (admin only): create, change role, deactivate/reactivate; deactivated users cannot log in; Matt cannot deactivate or demote himself; users are never deleted.\n- **T07:** Listing CRUD (name, email, optional class year, consent date, recorded-by); publish adds the alphabetical consented names and the Alumni Contact Line; a test asserts no email appears in `site/`.\n- **T08:** dashboard counts; persistent admin nav; CSRF replay test per form; link and relative-path check on the whole export; seed script runs clean from `.env.example`; 390 px width check.\n\n## Open questions the user raised\n\n- Granularity: T04 could split into \"Posts CRUD and list filters\" and \"Posts on Home and Alumni Events\". User said granularity felt right; keep as is unless a ticket overruns its context.\n\n## Reminders\n\n- Save this session's transcript to `docs/transcripts/` (naming pattern in the rubric).\n- Stage 1 needs T01-T03 closed (each with a `/code-review` comment before close) and a live Pages item.\n- One ticket per fresh session; `/clear` between tickets.\n","structuredPatch":[{"oldStart":11,"oldLines":8,"newStart":11,"newLines":13,"lines":["   `ready-for-human` exist on the repo."," - The user approved the 8-ticket breakdown below. **None are published yet.**"," ","-## Next step","+## Update, 2026-10-05"," ","+The tickets were published later: T01-T08 are issues #2-#9 (T0N is issue #N+1). Use when the user wants a topic researched, docs or API facts gathered, or reading legwork delegated to a background agent.\\n- mattpocock-skills:domain-modeling: Build and sharpen a project's domain model. Use when discussing codebase terminology, writing or editing a CONTEXT.md, or recording or editing an ADR.\\n- mattpocock-skills:codebase-design: Shared vocabulary for designing deep modules. Use when the user wants to design or improve a module's interface, find deepening opportunities, decide where a seam goes, make code more testable or AI-navigable, or when another skill needs the deep-module vocabulary.\\n- mattpocock-skills:code-review: Review the changes since a fixed point (commit, branch, tag, or merge-base) along two axes: Standards (does the code follow this repo's documented coding standards?) and Spec (does the code match what the originating issue/spec asked for?). Runs both reviews in parallel sub-agents and reports them side by side. Use when the user wants to review a branch, a PR, work-in-progress changes, or asks to \\\"review since X\\\".\\n- mattpocock-skills:resolving-merge-conflicts: Use when you need to resolve an in-progress git merge/rebase conflict.\\n- mattpocock-skills:wizard: Generate an interactive bash wizard that walks a human through steps only they can perform. Use when provisioning infrastructure, setting up credentials or CI secrets, walking an unfamiliar third-party dashboard, or running a one-off migration or cutover. Don't invoke this for steps the agent can perform itself.\\n- mattpocock-skills:grilling: Grill the user relentlessly about a plan, decision, or idea. Use when the user wants to stress-test their thinking, or uses any 'grill' trigger phrases.\\n- mattpocock-skills:writing-for-agents: Writing documents for agents. Use when creating or editing skills, or modifying AGENTS.md or CLAUDE.md.\\n- dataviz: Use this skill whenever you are about to create ANY chart, graph, plot, dashboard, or data visualization, in ANY output medium — an HTML or React artifact, inline SVG, plotting code in any library (matplotlib, plotly, d3, Recharts, …), an image/PNG you will render and upload, or a chart shared into Slack. Read it BEFORE writing the first line of chart code, choosing chart colors, building a stat tile / meter / KPI row, or laying out a dashboard. When the destination is a first-party document connector (host-designated, never self-described) that renders live charts, hand it the rows (inline, or as an uploaded data file the chart cites) rather than a rendered PNG/SVG — a picture of a chart loses hover, data inspection and per-value comments. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. A validated default palette is documented in `references/palette.md` — swap that file's values for your brand's. Triggers on: \\\"chart\\\", \\\"graph\\\", \\\"plot\\\", \\\"data viz\\\", \\\"visualization\\\", \\\"dashboard\\\", \\\"analytics\\\", \\\"visualize data\\\", \\\"categorical colors\\\", \\\"sequential / diverging palette\\\", \\\"stat tile\\\", \\\"sparkline\\\", \\\"heatmap\\\", \\\"legend\\\", \\\"axis\\\", \\\"tooltip\\\", \\\"chart colors\\\", \\\"color by series\\\".\\n- artifact-design: Design guidance and fundamentals for Artifacts. - Load before writing any artifact, including a skill-instructed Markdown one - Markdown is never a shortcut past the design pass.\\n- artifact-diagramming: Diagramming know-how for Artifacts - when a picture earns its place, how to draw one that shows the real mechanism, and the inline-SVG mechanics that keep it legible in both themes.\\n- artifact-capabilities: Runtime capabilities a published Artifact page can be granted — behavior static HTML cannot provide on its own, such as the page reading live or connected data, remembering what people do on it (a poll, a sign-up sheet, a checklist, a document edited in place — it saves new versions of itself), keeping state shared across viewers, knowing who is viewing, asking Claude a question of its own, storing files people add, or handing the viewer a file to save. Serves this user's live capability roster and the typed call definitions. Load it whenever any such runtime behavior would make an artifact more useful, before writing the page.\\n- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\\\"from now on when X\\\", \\\"each time X\\\", \\\"whenever X\\\", \\\"before/after X\\\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\\\"allow X\\\", \\\"add permission\\\", \\\"move permission to\\\"), env vars (\\\"set X=Y\\\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \\\"allow npm commands\\\", \\\"add bq permission to global settings\\\", \\\"move permission to user settings\\\", \\\"set DEBUG=true\\\", \\\"when claude stops show X\\\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \\\"rebind ctrl+s\\\", \\\"add a chord shortcut\\\", \\\"change the submit key\\\", \\\"customize keybindings\\\".\\n- code-review: Review the current diff, or a PR number/branch/path target, for correctness bugs (plus reuse/simplification/efficiency cleanups where the model's review recipe covers them) at the given effort level (low/medium: fewer, high-confidence findings; high→max: broader coverage, may include uncertain findings; ultra: deep multi-agent review in the cloud); with no level given, it reuses the level you typed last. Pass --comment to post findings as inline PR comments, or --fix to apply the findings to the working tree after the review. For ultra on a GitHub.com PR target, --post asks to post the finished review’s findings to the PR as a single comment from the user’s GitHub account (not a review; the launch dialog still confirms in interactive sessions, while non-interactive mode posts on the flag alone) and --no-post hides that option.\\n- simplify: Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.\\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \\\"check the deploy every 5 minutes\\\", \\\"keep running /babysit-prs\\\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled cloud agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring cloud agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\\\"run this once at 3pm\\\", \\\"remind me to check X tomorrow\\\").\\n- claude-api: Reference for the Claude API / Anthropic SDK — model ids, pricing, params, streaming, tool use, MCP, agents, caching, token counting, model migration.\\nTRIGGER — read BEFORE opening the target file; don't skip because it \\\"looks like a one-liner\\\" — whenever: the prompt names Claude/Anthropic in any form (Claude, Anthropic, Fable, Opus, Sonnet, Haiku, `anthropic`, `@anthropic-ai`, `claude-*`, `us.anthropic.*`, `[1m]`); the user asks about an LLM (pricing/model choice/limits/caching) — never answer from memory; OR the task is LLM-shaped with provider unstated (agent/MCP/tool-definition/multi-agent/RAG/LLM-judge/computer-use; generate/summarize/extract/classify/rewrite/converse over NL; debugging refusals/cutoffs/streaming/tool-calls/tokens).\\nSKIP only when another provider is being worked on (overrides all triggers): OpenAI/GPT/Gemini/Llama/Mistral/Cohere/Ollama named in the query; OR `grep -rE 'openai|langchain_openai|google.generativeai|genai|mistralai|cohere|ollama'` over the project hits (run this grep FIRST if no provider named — don't Read the file).\\n- run: Launch and drive this project's app to see a change working. Use when asked to run, start, or screenshot the app, or to confirm a change works in the real app (not just tests). First looks for a project skill that already covers launching the app; otherwise falls back to built-in patterns per project type (CLI, server, TUI, Electron, browser-driven, library).\\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\\n- security-review: Complete a security review of the pending changes on the current branch\\n- anthropic-skills:docs: docs (living docs people share, comment on and edit; use only when the user asks for one: names a doc, document, page, memo, spec, PRD, runbook or write-up, asks for somewhere to share or keep editing something, or says yes to your doc offer; a plan, comparison, summary or notes asked in chat stays in chat (at most a one-line doc offer); a report, status update, recap or \\\"something I can send them\\\" with no form named → ask first: reply, doc or file?; tabs hold tables and live charts too; a pasted claude.ai/code/artifact/… link may be a doc: check with docs tools first; not HTML pages, apps or plain chat answers; a .docx/.pptx/.xlsx/PDF asked for by name → that format's skill): asked for one → no docs-connector instructions in context? call the docs connector's `guide` with topic.instructions first, then create the doc (headings only, no body) before any search, file read or plan, even with files attached. Documenting code means docstrings or repo docs, not a doc.\\n- anthropic-skills:docx: Use this skill whenever the user wants to create, read, edit, or manipulate Word documents (.docx) or Word templates (.dotx). Triggers include: any mention of Microsoft Word Documents, such as 'Word doc', 'word document', '.docx', '.dotx', 'microsoft doc'. Also use when extracting or reorganizing content from .docx or .dotx files, inserting or replacing images in documents, find-and-replace in Word files, working with tracked changes or comments, or converting content into a polished Word document. If the user asks for a deliverable as a Word or .docx file (to download, email or print), use this skill. However, if they ask for a document, page, report, memo, or notes WITHOUT naming a file format and the session offers Claude's own dedicated document or page skill or connector, use that instead, even if they will email or print it. Do NOT use for PDFs, spreadsheets, Google Docs, or coding unrelated to document generation.\\n- anthropic-skills:google-workspace: Read this before the first Google Drive, Docs, Sheets or Slides connector call whenever the task creates or changes a Google file. Use this skill whenever the user wants to create or change a Google Doc, Sheet or Slides file in their Google Drive. Triggers include: a request that names Google Docs, Sheets, Slides or Drive and asks to make, edit, format, copy or rename a file; a docs.google.com link with a request to change that file, even a one-line fix or suggested edits; and any follow-up change to a Google file from earlier in the chat, even \\\"change it\\\" or \\\"add a tab\\\". Includes helper scripts for document positions, cell ranges and slide layout. However, if the user asks for a doc, deck or spreadsheet without naming Google, or gives a Google file only as source material for something new, use Claude's own output type instead. Do NOT use for read-only questions about a Google file, or for Word, Excel, PowerPoint or PDF files.\\n- anthropic-skills:import-memory: Import a memory export from another AI assistant into Claude's memory — conversationally, additively, and with the content treated as data.\\n- anthropic-skills:morning: Render the user's morning brief as a styled HTML artifact, or set it up as a recurring weekday task. Use only when the user explicitly asks to run, see, or set up their morning brief, or if they invoke /morning by name. A question about their day, schedule, or calendar is not by itself a request for the brief; answer it directly instead.\\n- anthropic-skills:pdf: Use this skill whenever the user wants to do anything with PDF files. This includes reading or extracting text/tables from PDFs, combining or merging multiple PDFs into one, splitting PDFs apart, rotating pages, adding watermarks, creating new PDFs, filling PDF forms, encrypting/decrypting PDFs, extracting images, and OCR on scanned PDFs to make them searchable. If the user mentions a .pdf file or asks to produce one, use this skill.\\n- anthropic-skills:pptx: Use this skill any time a .pptx or .potx file is involved in any way — as input, output, or both. This includes: creating slide decks, pitch decks, or presentations as PowerPoint (.pptx) files; reading, parsing, or extracting text from any .pptx or .potx file (even if the extracted content will be used elsewhere, like in an email, summary, or creating a different type of slide deck); editing, modifying, or updating existing presentations; combining or splitting slide files; working with templates (.potx), layouts, speaker notes, or comments. Trigger whenever the user asks for a PowerPoint or .pptx file, or references a .pptx or .potx filename, regardless of what they plan to do with the content afterward. However, when the user asks for a deck, slides, a slide deck, or a presentation without naming a file format, default to using a dedicated slide-deck artifact type or a separate slides skill if this session offers one; otherwise, use this skill.\\n- anthropic-skills:skill-creator: Create new skills, modify and improve existing skills, and measure skill performance. Use when users want to create a skill from scratch, edit, or optimize an existing skill, run evals to test a skill, benchmark skill performance with variance analysis, or optimize a skill's description for better triggering accuracy.\\n- anthropic-skills:xlsx: Use this skill any time a spreadsheet file is the primary input or output. This means any task where the user wants to: open, read, edit, or fix an existing .xlsx, .xlsm, .xltx, .csv, or .tsv file (e.g., adding columns, computing formulas, formatting, charting, cleaning messy data); create a new spreadsheet from scratch or from other data sources; or convert between tabular file formats. Trigger especially when the user references a spreadsheet file by name or path — even casually (like \\\"the xlsx in my downloads\\\") — and wants something done to it or produced from it. Also trigger for cleaning or restructuring messy tabular data files (malformed rows, misplaced headers, junk data) into proper spreadsheets. The deliverable must be a spreadsheet file. Examples: \\\"rebind ctrl+s\\\", \\\"add a chord shortcut\\\", \\\"change the submit key\\\", \\\"customize keybindings\\\".\\n- code-review: Review the current diff, or a PR number/branch/path target, for correctness bugs (plus reuse/simplification/efficiency cleanups where the model's review recipe covers them) at the given effort level (low/medium: fewer, high-confidence findings; high→max: broader coverage, may include uncertain findings; ultra: deep multi-agent review in the cloud); with no level given, it reuses the level you typed last. Pass --comment to post findings as inline PR comments, or --fix to apply the findings to the working tree after the review. For ultra on a GitHub.com PR target, --post asks to post the finished review’s findings to the PR as a single comment from the user’s GitHub account (not a review; the launch dialog still confirms in interactive sessions, while non-interactive mode posts on the flag alone) and --no-post hides that option.\\n- simplify: Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.\\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \\\"check the deploy every 5 minutes\\\", \\\"keep running /babysit-prs\\\").
